### Q77. MHT CET 2025 (22 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Which from following solutions exhibits minimum boiling point elevation under identical conditions?
(Assume complete dissociation)

**Options:**

- **(A)** 0.2 m KCl
- **(B)** 0.1 m NaCl
- **(C)** $1 \mathrm{~m} \mathrm{AlCl}_3$
- **(D)** $0.05 \mathrm{~m} \mathrm{MgCl}_2$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $0.05 \mathrm{~m} \mathrm{MgCl}_2$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Boiling point elevation is a colligative property, which depends on the total number of solute particles in the solution.

$\Delta T_b = i \times K_b \times m$

For complete dissociation, the van't Hoff factor $i$ is equal to the number of ions produced per formula unit.

For $0.2 \text{ m KCl}$: $i = 2$, $i \times m = 2 \times 0.2 = 0.4$

For $0.1 \text{ m NaCl}$: $i = 2$, $i \times m = 2 \times 0.1 = 0.2$

For $1 \text{ m AlCl}_3$: $i = 4$, $i \times m = 4 \times 1 = 4$

For $0.05 \text{ m MgCl}_2$: $i = 3$, $i \times m = 3 \times 0.05 = 0.15$

Since $0.05 \text{ m MgCl}_2$ has the minimum value of $i \times m$, it exhibits the minimum boiling point elevation.

Answer: $0.05 \mathrm{~m} \mathrm{MgCl}_2$

</details>

### Q78. MHT CET 2025 (22 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Calculate the relative lowering of vapour pressure of solution containing 3 g urea in 50 g water. [ molar mass of urea $=60 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$ ]

**Options:**

- **(A)** $0.013$
- **(B)** $0.025$
- **(C)** $0.018$
- **(D)** $0.028$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $0.018$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Mass of urea, $W_2 = 3$ g

Molar mass of urea, $M_2 = 60$ g mol$^{-1}$

Mass of water, $W_1 = 50$ g

Molar mass of water, $M_1 = 18$ g mol$^{-1}$

Relative lowering of vapour pressure is equal to the mole fraction of the solute:

$\dfrac{\Delta P}{P_1^0} = x_2 = \dfrac{n_2}{n_1 + n_2}$

For a dilute solution, $n_1 \gg n_2$, so $n_1 + n_2 \approx n_1$

$\dfrac{\Delta P}{P_1^0} \approx \dfrac{n_2}{n_1} = \dfrac{W_2 \times M_1}{M_2 \times W_1}$

Substituting the given values:

$\dfrac{\Delta P}{P_1^0} = \dfrac{3 \times 18}{60 \times 50} = \dfrac{54}{3000} = 0.018$

Answer: $0.018$

</details>

### Q79. MHT CET 2025 (22 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Calculate the number of moles of nonvolatile solute dissolved in 0.3 kg solvent if $\Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{b}}=0.3 \mathrm{~K}$ and $\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{b}}$ for solvent is $1.8 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $0.050$
- **(B)** $0.044$
- **(C)** $0.062$
- **(D)** $0.073$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $0.050$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The elevation in boiling point is given by the formula:

$\Delta T_b = K_b \times m$

where $m$ is the molality of the solution. Rearranging for molality:

$m = \dfrac{\Delta T_b}{K_b}$

Substituting the given values $\Delta T_b = 0.3 \text{ K}$ and $K_b = 1.8 \text{ K kg mol}^{-1}$:

$m = \dfrac{0.3}{1.8} = \dfrac{1}{6} \text{ mol kg}^{-1}$

Molality is also defined as the number of moles of solute ($n$) divided by the mass of the solvent in kg ($W$).

$m = \dfrac{n}{W}$

Rearranging to find the number of moles:

$n = m \times W$

Given the mass of the solvent $W = 0.3 \text{ kg}$:

$n = \dfrac{1}{6} \times 0.3 = 0.050 \text{ mol}$

Answer: $0.050$

</details>

### Q80. MHT CET 2025 (22 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Calculate the osmotic pressure of 0.5 M aqueous solution of nonvolatile solute at $300 \mathrm{~K}\left[\mathrm{R}=0.0821 \mathrm{~atm~} \mathrm{dm}^3 \mathrm{~K}^{-1} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}\right]$

**Options:**

- **(A)** 9.51 atm
- **(B)** 12.32 atm
- **(C)** 15.60 atm
- **(D)** 6.75 atm

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) 12.32 atm**

**Detailed Solution:**

The osmotic pressure $\pi$ of a solution is given by the formula:

$\pi = CRT$

Given:
Concentration $C = 0.5 \text{ M} = 0.5 \text{ mol dm}^{-3}$
Gas constant $R = 0.0821 \text{ atm dm}^3 \text{ K}^{-1} \text{ mol}^{-1}$
Temperature $T = 300 \text{ K}$

Substituting the values into the formula:

$\pi = 0.5 \times 0.0821 \times 300$

$\pi = 0.5 \times 24.63$

$\pi = 12.315 \text{ atm}$

Rounding to two decimal places, we get $12.32 \text{ atm}$.

Answer: 12.32 atm

</details>

### Q81. MHT CET 2025 (21 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Calculate the relative lowering of vapour pressure of solution containing 0.56 g nonvolatile solute in 100 g water [ molar mass of solute $=60 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$ ]

**Options:**

- **(A)** $0.0024$
- **(B)** $0.0017$
- **(C)** $0.0120$
- **(D)** $0.0221$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $0.0017$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Mass of solute, $W_2 = 0.56$ g

Molar mass of solute, $M_2 = 60$ g mol$^{-1}$

Number of moles of solute, $n_2 = \dfrac{W_2}{M_2} = \dfrac{0.56}{60} = 0.00933$ mol

Mass of solvent (water), $W_1 = 100$ g

Molar mass of solvent, $M_1 = 18$ g mol$^{-1}$

Number of moles of solvent, $n_1 = \dfrac{W_1}{M_1} = \dfrac{100}{18} = 5.555$ mol

Relative lowering of vapour pressure is equal to the mole fraction of the solute, $x_2$:

$x_2 = \dfrac{n_2}{n_1 + n_2}$

$x_2 = \dfrac{0.00933}{5.555 + 0.00933} = \dfrac{0.00933}{5.5643} \approx 0.001677$

Rounding off, the relative lowering of vapour pressure is $0.0017$.

Answer: $0.0017$

</details>

### Q82. MHT CET 2025 (21 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Arrange the following solutions according to decreasing order of osmotic pressure under similar condition of temperature and assuming complete dissociation
A. $0.2 \mathrm{~m~} \mathrm{KCl}$
B. $0.3 \mathrm{~m~} \mathrm{MgSO}_4$
C. $0.1 \mathrm{~m~} \mathrm{BaCl}_2$
D. $0.5 \mathrm{~m~} \mathrm{Al}_2\left(\mathrm{SO}_4\right)_3$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $D>B>A>C$
- **(B)** $\mathrm{C}>\mathrm{D}>\mathrm{A}>\mathrm{B}$
- **(C)** $\mathrm{A}>\mathrm{C}>\mathrm{D}>\mathrm{B}$
- **(D)** $\mathrm{B}>\mathrm{C}>\mathrm{A}>\mathrm{D}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $D>B>A>C$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Osmotic pressure is a colligative property, which depends on the total number of solute particles in the solution. For electrolytes, $\pi \propto i \times m$, where $i$ is the van't Hoff factor and $m$ is the molality.

For complete dissociation:
A. $\mathrm{KCl} \rightarrow \mathrm{K}^+ + \mathrm{Cl}^-$, $i = 2$. Effective concentration $= 2 \times 0.2 = 0.4 \mathrm{~m}$
B. $\mathrm{MgSO}_4 \rightarrow \mathrm{Mg}^{2+} + \mathrm{SO}_4^{2-}$, $i = 2$. Effective concentration $= 2 \times 0.3 = 0.6 \mathrm{~m}$
C. $\mathrm{BaCl}_2 \rightarrow \mathrm{Ba}^{2+} + 2\mathrm{Cl}^-$, $i = 3$. Effective concentration $= 3 \times 0.1 = 0.3 \mathrm{~m}$
D. $\mathrm{Al}_2(\mathrm{SO}_4)_3 \rightarrow 2\mathrm{Al}^{3+} + 3\mathrm{SO}_4^{2-}$, $i = 5$. Effective concentration $= 5 \times 0.5 = 2.5 \mathrm{~m}$

Comparing the effective concentrations, the decreasing order of osmotic pressure is $\mathrm{D} > \mathrm{B} > \mathrm{A} > \mathrm{C}$.

Answer: $D>B>A>C$

</details>

### Q83. MHT CET 2025 (21 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Calculate the boiling point elevation of solution if 15 g urea is dissolved in 1000 g water.
$\left[\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{b}} \text { for water }=0.52 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1} ; \text { molar mass of urea }=60 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}\right]$

**Options:**

- **(A)** 0.13 K
- **(B)** 0.24 K
- **(C)** 0.38 K
- **(D)** 0.54 K

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) 0.13 K**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given:
Mass of solute (urea), $W_2 = 15 \text{ g}$
Molar mass of solute, $M_2 = 60 \text{ g mol}^{-1}$
Mass of solvent (water), $W_1 = 1000 \text{ g} = 1 \text{ kg}$
Ebullioscopic constant, $K_b = 0.52 \text{ K kg mol}^{-1}$

The molality of the solution is given by:
$m = \dfrac{W_2}{M_2 \times W_1}$

Substituting the values:
$m = \dfrac{15}{60 \times 1} = 0.25 \text{ mol kg}^{-1}$

The boiling point elevation is given by:
$\Delta T_b = K_b \times m$

$\Delta T_b = 0.52 \times 0.25 = 0.13 \text{ K}$

Answer: 0.13 K

</details>

### Q84. MHT CET 2025 (21 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Calculate molality of the solution containing nonvolatile solute if boiling point elevation of the solution is $0.2 \mathrm{~K}\left[\mathrm{~K}_{\mathrm{b}}=0.52 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}\right]$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $0.162 \mathrm{~mol} \mathrm{~kg}^{-1}$
- **(B)** $0.281 \mathrm{~mol} \mathrm{~kg}{ }^{-1}$
- **(C)** $0.385 \mathrm{~mol} \mathrm{~kg}^{-1}$
- **(D)** $0.501 \mathrm{~mol} \mathrm{~kg}^{-1}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $0.385 \mathrm{~mol} \mathrm{~kg}^{-1}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The elevation in boiling point is given by the formula:

$\Delta T_b = K_b \times m$

where $\Delta T_b$ is the boiling point elevation, $K_b$ is the molal elevation constant, and $m$ is the molality of the solution.

Given:

$\Delta T_b = 0.2 \text{ K}$

$K_b = 0.52 \text{ K kg mol}^{-1}$

Substituting the values into the formula:

$0.2 = 0.52 \times m$

$m = \dfrac{0.2}{0.52}$

$m = \dfrac{20}{52} = \dfrac{5}{13}$

$m \approx 0.385 \text{ mol kg}^{-1}$

Answer: $0.385 \mathrm{~mol} \mathrm{~kg}^{-1}$

</details>

### Q85. MHT CET 2025 (21 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Calculate the temperature of 0.05 M sucrose solution in Kelvin if the osmotic pressure of the solution is 1.5 atm.
$\left[\mathrm{R}=0.0821 \mathrm{dm}^3 \mathrm{~atm} \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~K}^{-1} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}\right]$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $370.2$
- **(B)** $375.4$
- **(C)** $380.6$
- **(D)** $365.4$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $365.4$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The osmotic pressure of a solution is given by the formula:

$\pi = CRT$

Substituting the given values $\pi = 1.5 \text{ atm}$, $C = 0.05 \text{ M}$, and $R = 0.0821 \text{ dm}^3 \text{ atm K}^{-1} \text{ mol}^{-1}$:

$1.5 = 0.05 \times 0.0821 \times T$

$T = \dfrac{1.5}{0.05 \times 0.0821}$

$T = \dfrac{1.5}{0.004105}$

$T = 365.4 \text{ K}$

Answer: $365.4$

</details>

### Q86. MHT CET 2025 (21 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Which of the following aqueous solutions exhibits lowest freezing point depression assuming complete dissociation?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $0.1 \mathrm{~m~} \mathrm{NaCl}$
- **(B)** $0.05 \mathrm{~m~} \mathrm{MgSO}_4$
- **(C)** $0.08 \mathrm{~m~} \mathrm{AlPO}_4$
- **(D)** $0.06 \mathrm{~m~} \mathrm{Al}_2\left(\mathrm{SO}_4\right)_3$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $0.05 \mathrm{~m~} \mathrm{MgSO}_4$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The freezing point depression is a colligative property given by $\Delta T_f = i \times K_f \times m$, where $i$ is the van't Hoff factor and $m$ is the molality of the solution.

For a given solvent, $\Delta T_f \propto i \times m$. Assuming complete dissociation, we calculate $i \times m$ for each option:

For $0.1 \mathrm{~m} \mathrm{~NaCl}$:
$\mathrm{NaCl} \rightarrow \mathrm{Na}^+ + \mathrm{Cl}^-$
$i = 2$
$i \times m = 2 \times 0.1 = 0.2$

For $0.05 \mathrm{~m} \mathrm{~MgSO}_4$:
$\mathrm{MgSO}_4 \rightarrow \mathrm{Mg}^{2+} + \mathrm{SO}_4^{2-}$
$i = 2$
$i \times m = 2 \times 0.05 = 0.1$

For $0.08 \mathrm{~m} \mathrm{~AlPO}_4$:
$\mathrm{AlPO}_4 \rightarrow \mathrm{Al}^{3+} + \mathrm{PO}_4^{3-}$
$i = 2$
$i \times m = 2 \times 0.08 = 0.16$

For $0.06 \mathrm{~m} \mathrm{~Al}_2(\mathrm{SO}_4)_3$:
$\mathrm{Al}_2(\mathrm{SO}_4)_3 \rightarrow 2\mathrm{Al}^{3+} + 3\mathrm{SO}_4^{2-}$
$i = 5$
$i \times m = 5 \times 0.06 = 0.30$

The lowest value of $i \times m$ is $0.1$, which corresponds to $0.05 \mathrm{~m} \mathrm{~MgSO}_4$. Therefore, it exhibits the lowest freezing point depression.

Answer: $0.05 \mathrm{~m~} \mathrm{MgSO}_4$

</details>

---

### Q87. MHT CET 2025 (20 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Calculate the molality of nonvolatile solution if solution freezes at $-0.95^{\circ} \mathrm{C}\left[\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{f}}\right.$ for water $=1.86 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$, freezing point of water $\left.=0^{\circ} \mathrm{C}\right]$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $0.032 \mathrm{~mol} \mathrm{~kg}^{-1}$
- **(B)** $0.041 \mathrm{~mol} \mathrm{~kg}^{-1}$
- **(C)** $0.51 \mathrm{~mol} \mathrm{~kg}^{-1}$
- **(D)** $0.065 \mathrm{~mol} \mathrm{~kg}^{-1}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $0.51 \mathrm{~mol} \mathrm{~kg}^{-1}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The depression in freezing point is given by $\Delta T_f = T_f^{\circ} - T_f$

$\Delta T_f = 0^{\circ}\mathrm{C} - (-0.95^{\circ}\mathrm{C}) = 0.95^{\circ}\mathrm{C} = 0.95 \mathrm{~K}$

The relation between depression in freezing point and molality is $\Delta T_f = K_f \times m$

$m = \dfrac{\Delta T_f}{K_f}$

$m = \dfrac{0.95}{1.86}$

$m = 0.51 \mathrm{~mol} \mathrm{~kg}^{-1}$

Answer: $0.51 \mathrm{~mol} \mathrm{~kg}^{-1}$

</details>

### Q88. MHT CET 2025 (20 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Find the expected value of $\Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{b}}$ for $1 \mathrm{~m} \mathrm{~Al~Cl}_3$ solution in water if solution of nonelectrolyte of same concentration has $\Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{b}}$ value ' $x$ ' K

**Options:**

- **(A)** $x \mathrm{~K}$
- **(B)** $2 x \mathrm{~K}$
- **(C)** $3 x \mathrm{~K}$
- **(D)** $4 x \mathrm{~K}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $4 x \mathrm{~K}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

For a nonelectrolyte solution, the van 't Hoff factor $i = 1$.

The elevation in boiling point is given by $\Delta T_b = i K_b m$.

For the nonelectrolyte solution of concentration $1 \text{ m}$, $\Delta T_b = 1 \times K_b \times 1 = x \text{ K}$.

For $\text{AlCl}_3$, it dissociates in water as $\text{AlCl}_3 \rightarrow \text{Al}^{3+} + 3\text{Cl}^-$.

Assuming complete dissociation, the van 't Hoff factor $i = 4$.

For the $1 \text{ m}$ $\text{AlCl}_3$ solution, $\Delta T_b = i K_b m = 4 \times K_b \times 1 = 4x \text{ K}$.

Answer: $4 x \mathrm{~K}$

</details>

### Q89. MHT CET 2025 (20 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Calculate the osmotic pressure of 0.03 mole of non electrolyte solute dissolved in $0.1 \mathrm{dm}^3$ of water at $300 \mathrm{~K}\left[\mathrm{R}=0.0821 \mathrm{dm}^3 \mathrm{~atm} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1} \mathrm{~K}^{-1}\right]$

**Options:**

- **(A)** 7.4 atm
- **(B)** $6.4 \mathrm{~atm}$
- **(C)** 8.0 atm
- **(D)** 5.6 atm

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) 7.4 atm**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given:

$n = 0.03 \text{ mol}$

$V = 0.1 \text{ dm}^3$

$T = 300 \text{ K}$

$R = 0.0821 \text{ dm}^3 \text{ atm mol}^{-1} \text{ K}^{-1}$

The osmotic pressure $\pi$ is given by the formula:

$\pi = \dfrac{n}{V} R T$

Substituting the given values:

$\pi = \dfrac{0.03}{0.1} \times 0.0821 \times 300$

$\pi = 0.3 \times 24.63$

$\pi = 7.389 \text{ atm}$

Rounding off, we get $\pi \approx 7.4 \text{ atm}$.

Answer: 7.4 atm

</details>

### Q90. MHT CET 2025 (20 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Which of the following solutions exhibits highest freezing point depression?

**Options:**

- **(A)** 0.1 m NaCl
- **(B)** $0.05 \mathrm{~m} \mathrm{MgSO}_4$
- **(C)** $1 \mathrm{~m} \mathrm{AlPO}_4$
- **(D)** $0.05 \mathrm{~m} \mathrm{Al}_2\left(\mathrm{SO}_4\right)_3$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $1 \mathrm{~m} \mathrm{AlPO}_4$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Depression in freezing point, $\Delta T_f = i K_f m$. For the same solvent, $\Delta T_f$ is proportional to $i \times m$.

$0.1 \text{ m NaCl}$: $\mathrm{NaCl} \rightarrow \mathrm{Na}^{+} + \mathrm{Cl}^{-}$, $i = 2$, so $i \times m = 2 \times 0.1 = 0.2$

$0.05 \text{ m } \mathrm{MgSO_4}$: $\mathrm{MgSO_4} \rightarrow \mathrm{Mg}^{2+} + \mathrm{SO_4}^{2-}$, $i = 2$, so $i \times m = 2 \times 0.05 = 0.1$

$1 \text{ m } \mathrm{AlPO_4}$: $\mathrm{AlPO_4} \rightarrow \mathrm{Al}^{3+} + \mathrm{PO_4}^{3-}$, $i = 2$, so $i \times m = 2 \times 1 = 2$

$0.05 \text{ m } \mathrm{Al_2(SO_4)_3}$: $\mathrm{Al_2(SO_4)_3} \rightarrow 2\mathrm{Al}^{3+} + 3\mathrm{SO_4}^{2-}$, $i = 5$, so $i \times m = 5 \times 0.05 = 0.25$

The value of $i \times m$ is highest for $1 \text{ m } \mathrm{AlPO_4}$, so it exhibits the highest depression in freezing point.

</details>

### Q92. MHT CET 2025 (19 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Calculate the molal elevation constant of solvent if boiling point of 0.12 m solution is 319.8 K (Boling point of solvent $=319.5 \mathrm{~K}$ )

**Options:**

- **(A)** $2.0 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(B)** $3.0 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(C)** $2.5 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(D)** $3.5 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $2.5 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Elevation in boiling point is given by $\Delta T_b = T_b - T_b^0$

Substituting the given values:

$\Delta T_b = 319.8 - 319.5 = 0.3 \mathrm{~K}$

The relation between elevation in boiling point and molality is $\Delta T_b = K_b \times m$

Rearranging for molal elevation constant $K_b$:

$K_b = \dfrac{\Delta T_b}{m}$

$K_b = \dfrac{0.3}{0.12} = 2.5 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$

Answer: $2.5 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$

</details>

### Q93. MHT CET 2025 (19 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Find molar mass of nonvolatile solute when 20 g of it dissolved in 200 g
water at 300 K .
[Relative lowering of vapour pressure $=0.02$ ]

**Options:**

- **(A)** $120 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(B)** $110 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(C)** $90 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(D)** $100 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $90 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The relative lowering of vapour pressure for a dilute solution is given by the formula:

$\dfrac{\Delta P}{P^0} = \dfrac{W_2 M_1}{M_2 W_1}$

where:
$W_2 = 20 \text{ g}$ (mass of solute)
$W_1 = 200 \text{ g}$ (mass of solvent, water)
$M_1 = 18 \text{ g mol}^{-1}$ (molar mass of water)
$\dfrac{\Delta P}{P^0} = 0.02$ (relative lowering of vapour pressure)

Substituting the given values into the formula:

$0.02 = \dfrac{20 \times 18}{M_2 \times 200}$

$0.02 = \dfrac{360}{200 M_2}$

$0.02 = \dfrac{1.8}{M_2}$

$M_2 = \dfrac{1.8}{0.02} = 90 \text{ g mol}^{-1}$

Answer: $90 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$

</details>

### Q94. MHT CET 2025 (19 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Calculate the percent dissociation of 0.02 m solution if its freezing point
depression is 0.046 K
$\left[\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{f}}\right.$ for water $\left.=1.86 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1} ; \mathrm{n}=2\right]$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $12.3 \%$
- **(B)** $23.6 \%$
- **(C)** $35.00 \%$
- **(D)** $48.1 \%$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $23.6 \%$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The formula for freezing point depression is:

$\Delta T_f = i \times K_f \times m$

Substituting the given values:

$0.046 = i \times 1.86 \times 0.02$

$i = \dfrac{0.046}{0.0372} \approx 1.2365$

The degree of dissociation $\alpha$ is related to the van 't Hoff factor $i$ by the formula:

$\alpha = \dfrac{i - 1}{n - 1}$

Given $n = 2$, we get:

$\alpha = \dfrac{1.2365 - 1}{2 - 1} = 0.2365$

Percent dissociation $= \alpha \times 100 = 0.2365 \times 100 = 23.65 \%$

The closest given option is $23.6 \%$.

Answer: $23.6 \%$

</details>

### Q98. MHT CET 2024 (16 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Calculate van't Hoff factor of 0.15 m solution of electrolyte if it freezes at -0.5 $^\circ$C.
$\left[\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{f}}=1.86 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}\right]$

**Options:**

- **(A)** 1.12
- **(B)** 1.25
- **(C)** 1.45
- **(D)** 1.79

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) 1.79**

**Detailed Solution:**

The depression in freezing point is given by the formula:

$\Delta T_f = i \times K_f \times m$

Given:
$\Delta T_f = 0^\circ\text{C} - (-0.5^\circ\text{C}) = 0.5 \text{ K}$
$K_f = 1.86 \text{ K kg mol}^{-1}$
$m = 0.15 \text{ m}$

Substituting the values into the formula:

$0.5 = i \times 1.86 \times 0.15$

$0.5 = i \times 0.279$

$i = \dfrac{0.5}{0.279} \approx 1.79$

Answer: 1.79

</details>

### Q99. MHT CET 2024 (16 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Identify the correct statement from following properties.

**Options:**

- **(A)** The osmosis is a colligative property.
- **(B)** The vapour pressure of solution containing nonvolatile solute is lower than that of pure solvent at any given temperature.
- **(C)** The osmotic pressure of 0.1 M NaCl solution is lower than 0.1 M sucrose solution.
- **(D)** The boiling point of solution containing nonvolatile solute is lower than that of pure solvent.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) The vapour pressure of solution containing nonvolatile solute is lower than that of pure solvent at any given temperature.**

**Detailed Solution:**

Osmosis is a process, whereas osmotic pressure is a colligative property. Thus, the first statement is incorrect.

When a nonvolatile solute is added to a pure solvent, the surface area available for the escape of solvent molecules decreases, which lowers the vapour pressure of the solution compared to the pure solvent at any given temperature. Thus, the second statement is correct.

NaCl is an electrolyte and dissociates into two ions ($i \approx 2$), while sucrose is a nonelectrolyte ($i = 1$). Therefore, the osmotic pressure of $0.1$ M NaCl is higher than that of $0.1$ M sucrose. Thus, the third statement is incorrect.

The addition of a nonvolatile solute lowers the vapour pressure, which means a higher temperature is required to make the vapour pressure equal to the atmospheric pressure. Hence, the boiling point of the solution is higher than that of the pure solvent (boiling point elevation). Thus, the fourth statement is incorrect.

Answer: The vapour pressure of solution containing nonvolatile solute is lower than that of pure solvent at any given temperature.

</details>

---

### Q100. MHT CET 2024 (15 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

What is the vapour pressure of a solution containing 0.1 mol of non volatile solute dissolved in 16.2 g water? $\left(\mathrm{P}_1^0=24 \mathrm{mmHg}\right.$, molar mass of water $18 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$ )

**Options:**

- **(A)** 12.4 mmHg
- **(B)** 18.1 mm Hg
- **(C)** 15.7 mmHg
- **(D)** $21.3 \mathrm{mmHg}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $21.3 \mathrm{mmHg}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Moles of water, $n_1 = \dfrac{W_1}{M_1} = \dfrac{16.2}{18} = 0.9$ mol

Moles of solute, $n_2 = 0.1$ mol

Using the approximate formula for relative lowering of vapour pressure (commonly used for dilute solutions):

$\dfrac{P_1^0 - P}{P_1^0} = \dfrac{n_2}{n_1}$

Substituting the given values:

$\dfrac{24 - P}{24} = \dfrac{0.1}{0.9}$

$24 - P = \dfrac{24}{9}$

$24 - P = 2.67$

$P = 24 - 2.67 = 21.33 \text{ mmHg}$

Answer: $21.3 \mathrm{mmHg}$

</details>

### Q102. MHT CET 2024 (15 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Calculate molar mass of a solute at 300 K if 400 mg of it is dissolved in 300 mL of water exerts osmotic pressure of 0.2 atm .
$\left(\mathrm{R}=0.0821 \mathrm{~L} \mathrm{~atm} \mathrm{~K}^{-1} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}\right)$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $90 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(B)** $120 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(C)** $164 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(D)** $180 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $164 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The formula for osmotic pressure is given by:

$\pi = \dfrac{W R T}{M V}$

Rearranging the formula to solve for the molar mass $M$ of the solute:

$M = \dfrac{W R T}{\pi V}$

Given values are:
$W = 400 \text{ mg} = 0.4 \text{ g}$
$V = 300 \text{ mL} = 0.3 \text{ L}$
$\pi = 0.2 \text{ atm}$
$T = 300 \text{ K}$
$R = 0.0821 \text{ L atm K}^{-1} \text{ mol}^{-1}$

Substituting the values into the formula:

$M = \dfrac{0.4 \times 0.0821 \times 300}{0.2 \times 0.3}$

$M = \left(\dfrac{0.4}{0.2}\right) \times \left(\dfrac{300}{0.3}\right) \times 0.0821$

$M = 2 \times 1000 \times 0.0821$

$M = 164.2 \text{ g mol}^{-1}$

Approximating to the nearest integer, we get $164 \text{ g mol}^{-1}$.

Answer: $164 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$

</details>

---

### Q103. MHT CET 2024 (15 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

What is the expected value of $\Delta T_f$ for $1.25 \mathrm{~m} \mathrm{CaCl}_2$ solution if 1.25 m sucrose solution has $\Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{f}}$ value $\mathrm{x} K$ ?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $x$ K
- **(B)** $\frac{x}{2} \mathrm{~K}$
- **(C)** $2 x \mathrm{K}$
- **(D)** $3 x$ K

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $3 x$ K**

**Detailed Solution:**

The depression in freezing point is given by the formula:

$\Delta T_f = i \times K_f \times m$

For sucrose, which is a non-electrolyte, the van't Hoff factor $i = 1$.

$\Delta T_f (\text{sucrose}) = 1 \times K_f \times 1.25 = x$

For $\text{CaCl}_2$, it is a strong electrolyte and dissociates into three ions (one $\text{Ca}^{2+}$ and two $\text{Cl}^{-}$), so the van't Hoff factor $i = 3$.

$\Delta T_f (\text{CaCl}_2) = 3 \times K_f \times 1.25$

Substituting the value of $K_f \times 1.25$ from the sucrose equation:

$\Delta T_f (\text{CaCl}_2) = 3 \times x = 3x \text{ K}$

Answer: $3 x$ K

</details>

---

### Q104. MHT CET 2024 (11 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

In a solution, mole fraction of solute is 0.2 , when lowering in vapour pressure is 10 mm Hg . To get lowering of vapour pressure of 20 mm Hg , mole fraction of solute in solution is

**Options:**

- **(A)** 0.2
- **(B)** 0.4
- **(C)** 0.6
- **(D)** 0.8

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) 0.4**

**Detailed Solution:**

According to Raoult's law, the relative lowering of vapour pressure is equal to the mole fraction of the solute.

$\dfrac{\Delta P}{P^0} = x_2$

For the first solution:

$\dfrac{10}{P^0} = 0.2$

$P^0 = \dfrac{10}{0.2} = 50 \text{ mm Hg}$

For the second solution, the lowering of vapour pressure is $20 \text{ mm Hg}$.

$\dfrac{20}{P^0} = x_2'$

$x_2' = \dfrac{20}{50} = 0.4$

Answer: 0.4

</details>

---

### Q105. MHT CET 2024 (11 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Vapour pressure of a pure solvent is 550 mm of Hg . By addition of a non volatile solute it decreases to 510 mm of Hg . Calculate the mole fraction of solute in solution.

**Options:**

- **(A)** 0.215
- **(B)** 0.072
- **(C)** 0.610
- **(D)** 0.512

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) 0.072**

**Detailed Solution:**

According to Raoult's law, the relative lowering of vapour pressure is equal to the mole fraction of the solute.

$\dfrac{P_1^0 - P_1}{P_1^0} = x_2$

Given:
$P_1^0 = 550$ mm Hg
$P_1 = 510$ mm Hg

Substituting the values:
$x_2 = \dfrac{550 - 510}{550}$

$x_2 = \dfrac{40}{550}$

$x_2 = \dfrac{4}{55} \approx 0.072$

Answer: 0.072

</details>

---

### Q106. MHT CET 2024 (11 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Calculate van't Hoff factor (i) of 0.2 m aqueous solution of an electrolyte if it freezes at $-0.660^{\circ} \mathrm{C} .\left(\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{f}}=1.84 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}\right)$

**Options:**

- **(A)** 1.97
- **(B)** 1.79
- **(C)** 0.242
- **(D)** 0.557

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) 1.79**

**Detailed Solution:**

The depression in freezing point is given by:

$\Delta T_f = T_f^{\circ} - T_f = 0^{\circ}\mathrm{C} - (-0.660^{\circ}\mathrm{C}) = 0.660 \mathrm{~K}$

The formula relating depression in freezing point to the van't Hoff factor is:

$\Delta T_f = i \times K_f \times m$

Substituting the given values:

$0.660 = i \times 1.84 \times 0.2$

$0.660 = i \times 0.368$

$i = \dfrac{0.660}{0.368}$

$i \approx 1.79$

Answer: 1.79

</details>

### Q107. MHT CET 2024 (10 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Calculate molar mass of nonvolatile solute if a solution containing 0.35 g solute in 100 g .water has boiling point elevation 0.01 K $\left[\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{b}}=0.50 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}\right.$ ]

**Options:**

- **(A)** $265 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(B)** $175 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(C)** $105 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(D)** $195 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $175 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given:
Mass of solute, $w_2 = 0.35$ g
Mass of solvent, $w_1 = 100$ g $= 0.1$ kg
Elevation in boiling point, $\Delta T_b = 0.01$ K
Molal elevation constant, $K_b = 0.50$ K kg mol$^{-1}$

The formula for elevation in boiling point is:
$\Delta T_b = K_b \times m$

Substituting the expression for molality $m$:
$\Delta T_b = \dfrac{K_b \times w_2}{M_2 \times w_1 \text{ (in kg)}}$

Rearranging to solve for the molar mass of the solute $M_2$:
$M_2 = \dfrac{K_b \times w_2}{\Delta T_b \times w_1 \text{ (in kg)}}$

Substituting the given values:
$M_2 = \dfrac{0.50 \times 0.35}{0.01 \times 0.1}$

$M_2 = \dfrac{0.175}{0.001} = 175 \text{ g mol}^{-1}$

Answer: $175 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$

</details>

---

### Q108. MHT CET 2024 (10 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

0.1 molal aqueous solution of glucose boils at $100.16^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$. What is boiling point of 0.5 molal aqueous solution of glucose?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $100.80^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$
- **(B)** $100.16^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$
- **(C)** $100.10^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$
- **(D)** $20.8^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $100.80^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The elevation in boiling point for the $0.1$ molal solution is $\Delta T_{b1} = 100.16^{\circ} \mathrm{C} - 100^{\circ} \mathrm{C} = 0.16^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$.

Using the formula $\Delta T_b = K_b \times m$, the elevation in boiling point is directly proportional to the molality of the solution.

$\dfrac{\Delta T_{b2}}{\Delta T_{b1}} = \dfrac{m_2}{m_1}$

$\dfrac{\Delta T_{b2}}{0.16} = \dfrac{0.5}{0.1} = 5$

$\Delta T_{b2} = 5 \times 0.16 = 0.80^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$

The boiling point of the $0.5$ molal solution is $100^{\circ} \mathrm{C} + 0.80^{\circ} \mathrm{C} = 100.80^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$.

Answer: $100.80^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$

</details>

---

### Q109. MHT CET 2024 (10 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

0.2 molal aqueous solution of KCl freezes at $-0.680^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$. Calculate van't Hoff factor for this solution. $\left(\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{f}}=1.86 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}\right)$

- **(B)** 1.32
- **(C)** 1.42
- **(D)** 1.83

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) 1.83**

**Detailed Solution:**

The depression in freezing point is given by the formula:

$\Delta T_f = i \times K_f \times m$

Given:
$\Delta T_f = 0^{\circ}\mathrm{C} - (-0.680^{\circ}\mathrm{C}) = 0.680 \mathrm{~K}$
$K_f = 1.86 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
$m = 0.2 \mathrm{~m}$

Substituting the values into the formula:

$0.680 = i \times 1.86 \times 0.2$

$0.680 = i \times 0.372$

$i = \dfrac{0.680}{0.372} \approx 1.83$

Answer: 1.83

### Q110. MHT CET 2024 (09 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Assuming complete ionisation, arrange the following solutions in order of increasing osmotic pressure.
a) $0.5 \mathrm{~m} \mathrm{Li}_2 \mathrm{SO}_4$.
b) $\quad 0.5 \mathrm{mKCl}$
c) $\quad 0.5 \mathrm{~m} \mathrm{Al}_2\left(\mathrm{SO}_4\right)_3$
d) $0.1 \mathrm{~m} \mathrm{BaCl}_2$

**Options:**

- **(A)** d $ \lt $ b $ \lt $ a $ \lt $ c
- **(B)** b $ \lt $ d $ \lt $ c $ \lt $ a
- **(C)** a $ \lt $ d $ \lt $ c $ \lt $ b
- **(D)** c $ \lt $ d $ \lt $ b $ \lt $ a

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) d $ \lt $ b $ \lt $ a $ \lt $ c**

**Detailed Solution:**

Osmotic pressure is a colligative property given by $\pi = iCRT$. Assuming molality is approximately equal to molarity for these solutions, $\pi \propto i \times m$, where $i$ is the van't Hoff factor and $m$ is the molality.

For $0.5 \text{ m } \text{Li}_2\text{SO}_4$, $i = 3$ (since it dissociates into $2\text{Li}^+$ and $\text{SO}_4^{2-}$).
$i \times m = 3 \times 0.5 = 1.5$

For $0.5 \text{ m } \text{KCl}$, $i = 2$ (since it dissociates into $\text{K}^+$ and $\text{Cl}^-$).
$i \times m = 2 \times 0.5 = 1.0$

For $0.5 \text{ m } \text{Al}_2(\text{SO}_4)_3$, $i = 5$ (since it dissociates into $2\text{Al}^{3+}$ and $3\text{SO}_4^{2-}$).
$i \times m = 5 \times 0.5 = 2.5$

For $0.1 \text{ m } \text{BaCl}_2$, $i = 3$ (since it dissociates into $\text{Ba}^{2+}$ and $2\text{Cl}^-$).
$i \times m = 3 \times 0.1 = 0.3$

Comparing the effective concentrations ($i \times m$), the increasing order is $0.3 < 1.0 < 1.5 < 2.5$.

Therefore, the increasing order of osmotic pressure is d $<$ b $<$ a $<$ c.

Answer: d $ \lt $ b $ \lt $ a $ \lt $ c

</details>

---

### Q111. MHT CET 2024 (09 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Calculate the molar mass of solute in a solution prepared by dissolving 1 gram in $0.3 \mathrm{dm}^3$ solvent having osmotic pressure 0.2 atm at 300 K.
$\left[\mathrm{R}=0.082 \mathrm{dm}^3 \mathrm{~atm} \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}\right]$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\quad 442 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(B)** $372 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(C)** $390 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(D)** $\quad 410 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $\quad 410 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The osmotic pressure of a solution is given by the formula:

$\pi = C R T = \dfrac{n}{V} R T = \dfrac{W}{M V} R T$

Rearranging the formula to solve for the molar mass $M$ of the solute:

$M = \dfrac{W R T}{\pi V}$

Substituting the given values $W = 1 \text{ g}$, $R = 0.082 \text{ dm}^3 \text{ atm K}^{-1} \text{ mol}^{-1}$, $T = 300 \text{ K}$, $\pi = 0.2 \text{ atm}$, and $V = 0.3 \text{ dm}^3$:

$M = \dfrac{1 \times 0.082 \times 300}{0.2 \times 0.3}$

$M = \dfrac{24.6}{0.06}$

$M = 410 \text{ g mol}^{-1}$

Answer: $\quad 410 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$

</details>

### Q113. MHT CET 2024 (09 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

Calculate the cryoscopic constant of solvent when 2.5 gram solute is dissolved in 35 gram solvent lowers its freezing point by 3 K . (molar mass of solute is $117 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$ )

**Options:**

- **(A)** $3.11 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(B)** $3.56 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(C)** $5.52 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(D)** $\quad 4.91 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $\quad 4.91 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The depression in freezing point is given by the formula:

$\Delta T_f = K_f \times m$

where $m$ is the molality of the solution, which can be expressed as:

$m = \dfrac{w_2 \times 1000}{M_2 \times w_1}$

Substituting the given values:

$3 = K_f \times \dfrac{2.5 \times 1000}{117 \times 35}$

$K_f = \dfrac{3 \times 117 \times 35}{2500}$

$K_f = \dfrac{12285}{2500} = 4.914 \mathrm{~K~kg~mol^{-1}}$

Answer: $\quad 4.91 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$

</details>

### Q115. MHT CET 2024 (04 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Which of the following solutions on complete dissociation exhibits maximum elevation in boiling point?

**Options:**

- **(A)** 0.1 m KCl
- **(B)** 0.05 m NaCl
- **(C)** $0.1 \mathrm{~m} \mathrm{BaCl}_2$
- **(D)** 0.1 m $MgSO_4$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $0.1 \mathrm{~m} \mathrm{BaCl}_2$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Elevation in boiling point is given by $\Delta T_b = i \cdot K_b \cdot m$.

Since $K_b$ is constant, $\Delta T_b \propto i \cdot m$.

For $0.1 \text{ m KCl}$, $i = 2$, so $i \cdot m = 2 \times 0.1 = 0.2$.

For $0.05 \text{ m NaCl}$, $i = 2$, so $i \cdot m = 2 \times 0.05 = 0.1$.

For $0.1 \text{ m BaCl}_2$, $i = 3$, so $i \cdot m = 3 \times 0.1 = 0.3$.

For $0.1 \text{ m MgSO}_4$, $i = 2$, so $i \cdot m = 2 \times 0.1 = 0.2$.

The maximum value of $i \cdot m$ is $0.3$ for $0.1 \text{ m BaCl}_2$, hence it exhibits the maximum elevation in boiling point.

Answer: $0.1 \mathrm{~m} \mathrm{BaCl}_2$

</details>

### Q116. MHT CET 2024 (04 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Calculate the molar mass of non volatile solute when 1 g of it is dissolved in 100 g solvent decreases its freezing point by 0.2 K .
$\left[\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{f}}=1.2 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}\right.$ ]

**Options:**

- **(A)** $55 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(B)** $60 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(C)** $65 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(D)** $70 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $60 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The depression in freezing point is given by the formula:

$\Delta T_f = K_f \times m$

where $m$ is the molality of the solution.

Molality $m = \dfrac{w_2 \times 1000}{M_2 \times w_1}$

Substituting the given values ($w_2 = 1 \text{ g}$, $w_1 = 100 \text{ g}$, $\Delta T_f = 0.2 \text{ K}$, $K_f = 1.2 \text{ K kg mol}^{-1}$):

$0.2 = \dfrac{1.2 \times 1 \times 1000}{M_2 \times 100}$

$0.2 = \dfrac{12}{M_2}$

$M_2 = \dfrac{12}{0.2} = 60 \text{ g mol}^{-1}$

Answer: $60 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$

</details>

### Q117. MHT CET 2024 (03 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Which of the following equation correctly represents molar mass of a solute by knowing boiling point elevation?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\mathrm{M}_2=\dfrac{1000 \times \Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{b}} \times \mathrm{W}_2}{\mathrm{~K}_{\mathrm{b}} \times \mathrm{W}_{\mathrm{i}}}$
- **(B)** $\mathrm{M}_2=\dfrac{1000 \times \mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{b}} \times \mathrm{W}_1}{\Delta \mathrm{~T}_{\mathrm{b}} \times \mathrm{W}_2}$
- **(C)** $\mathrm{M}_2=\dfrac{1000 \times \Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{b}} \times \mathrm{W}_1}{\mathrm{~K}_{\mathrm{b}} \times \mathrm{W}_2}$
- **(D)** $\mathrm{M}_2=\dfrac{1000 \times \mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{b}} \times \mathrm{W}_2}{\Delta \mathrm{~T}_{\mathrm{b}} \times \mathrm{W}_1}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $\mathrm{M}_2=\dfrac{1000 \times \mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{b}} \times \mathrm{W}_2}{\Delta \mathrm{~T}_{\mathrm{b}} \times \mathrm{W}_1}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The elevation in boiling point is given by the relation:

$\Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{b}} = \mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{b}} \times m$

where $m$ is the molality of the solution.

Molality is defined as the number of moles of solute dissolved per $1000 \text{ g}$ of solvent:

$m = \dfrac{\mathrm{W}_2 \times 1000}{\mathrm{M}_2 \times \mathrm{W}_1}$

where $\mathrm{W}_2$ is the mass of the solute, $\mathrm{M}_2$ is the molar mass of the solute, and $\mathrm{W}_1$ is the mass of the solvent.

Substituting the expression for molality into the boiling point elevation equation:

$\Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{b}} = \mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{b}} \times \dfrac{\mathrm{W}_2 \times 1000}{\mathrm{M}_2 \times \mathrm{W}_1}$

Rearranging the equation to solve for the molar mass of the solute $\mathrm{M}_2$:

$\mathrm{M}_2 = \dfrac{1000 \times \mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{b}} \times \mathrm{W}_2}{\Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{b}} \times \mathrm{W}_1}$

Answer: $\mathrm{M}_2=\dfrac{1000 \times \mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{b}} \times \mathrm{W}_2}{\Delta \mathrm{~T}_{\mathrm{b}} \times \mathrm{W}_1}$

</details>

### Q118. MHT CET 2024 (03 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

What mass of solute (molar mass $58 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$ ) is to be dissolved in $2.5 \mathrm{dm}^3 \mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}$ to generate osmotic pressure of 0.245 atm at 300 K ? $\left(\mathrm{R}=0.0821 \mathrm{dm}^3 \mathrm{~atm} \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}\right)$.

**Options:**

- **(A)** 1.0 gram
- **(B)** 0.72 gram
- **(C)** 1.44 gram
- **(D)** 1.75 gram

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) 1.44 gram**

**Detailed Solution:**

The osmotic pressure is given by the formula:

$\pi = \dfrac{w}{M V} R T$

Rearranging for the mass of the solute $w$:

$w = \dfrac{\pi M V}{R T}$

Substituting the given values:

$w = \dfrac{0.245 \times 58 \times 2.5}{0.0821 \times 300}$

$w = \dfrac{35.525}{24.63}$

$w = 1.44 \text{ g}$

Answer: 1.44 gram

</details>

---

### Q119. MHT CET 2024 (03 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The molal elevation boiling point constant for water is $0,513^{\circ} \mathrm{C} \mathrm{Kg} \mathrm{mol}^{-1}$.
Calculate boiling point of solution if 0.1 mole of sugar is dissolved in 200 g water?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $100.513^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$
- **(B)** $100.256^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$
- **(C)** $100.0513^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$
- **(D)** $100.025^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $100.256^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Molality of the solution is given by $m = \dfrac{\text{Moles of solute}}{\text{Mass of solvent in kg}}$

$m = \dfrac{0.1}{0.2} = 0.5 \text{ mol kg}^{-1}$

Elevation in boiling point is $\Delta T_b = K_b \times m$

$\Delta T_b = 0.513 \times 0.5 = 0.2565^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$

Boiling point of the solution is $T_b = 100^{\circ} \mathrm{C} + 0.2565^{\circ} \mathrm{C} = 100.2565^{\circ} \mathrm{C} \approx 100.256^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$

Answer: $100.256^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$

</details>

### Q120. MHT CET 2024 (03 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Calculate the molar mass of nonvolatile solute when 1.5 g of it is dissolved in 90 g solvent decreases its freezing point by 0.25 K .
$\left[\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{f}}=1.2 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}\right]$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $72 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(B)** $80 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(C)** $88 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(D)** $96 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $80 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given:
Mass of solute, $w_2 = 1.5 \text{ g}$
Mass of solvent, $w_1 = 90 \text{ g}$
Depression in freezing point, $\Delta T_f = 0.25 \text{ K}$
Cryoscopic constant, $K_f = 1.2 \text{ K kg mol}^{-1}$

The formula for depression in freezing point is:
$\Delta T_f = K_f \times m$

Substituting the expression for molality $m$:
$\Delta T_f = \dfrac{K_f \times w_2 \times 1000}{M_2 \times w_1}$

Rearranging for molar mass $M_2$:
$M_2 = \dfrac{K_f \times w_2 \times 1000}{\Delta T_f \times w_1}$

Substituting the given values:
$M_2 = \dfrac{1.2 \times 1.5 \times 1000}{0.25 \times 90}$

$M_2 = \dfrac{1800}{22.5} = 80 \text{ g mol}^{-1}$

Answer: $80 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$

</details>

### Q121. MHT CET 2024 (03 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Which from the following statements is correct for aqueous solution of $6 \mathrm{gL}^{-1}$ urea and $17 \cdot 12 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~L}^{-1}$ of sucrose?
[Molar mass of urea $=60 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
Molar mass of sucrose $=342 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$ ]

**Options:**

- **(A)** Osmotic pressure exhibited by urea solution is lower than that of sucrose solution.
- **(B)** Urea solution is hypertonic to sucrose solution,
- **(C)** These solutions are isotonic.
- **(D)** On doubling the concentration of sucrose solution it becomes hypertonic to urea solution.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) Urea solution is hypertonic to sucrose solution,**

**Detailed Solution:**

Concentration of urea solution, $C_{1} = \dfrac{6}{60} = 0.1 \text{ mol L}^{-1}$

Concentration of sucrose solution, $C_{2} = \dfrac{17.12}{342} \approx 0.05 \text{ mol L}^{-1}$

Osmotic pressure is given by $\pi = CRT$.

Since $C_{1} > C_{2}$, we have $\pi_{1} > \pi_{2}$.

A solution with higher osmotic pressure is hypertonic to the other solution. Therefore, the urea solution is hypertonic to the sucrose solution.

Answer: Urea solution is hypertonic to sucrose solution,

</details>

---

### Q122. MHT CET 2024 (03 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A solution of non volatile solute has boiling point elevation 1.75 K .
Calculate molality of solution $\left[\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{b}}=3.5 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}\right]$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $0.77 \mathrm{~mol} \mathrm{~kg}^{-1}$
- **(B)** $0.69 \mathrm{~mol} \mathrm{~kg}^{-1}$
- **(C)** $0.50 \mathrm{~mol} \mathrm{~kg}^{-1}$
- **(D)** $0.35 \mathrm{~mol} \mathrm{~kg}^{-1}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $0.50 \mathrm{~mol} \mathrm{~kg}^{-1}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The elevation in boiling point is given by:

$\Delta T_b = K_b \times m$

Substituting the given values:

$1.75 = 3.5 \times m$

$m = \dfrac{1.75}{3.5}$

$m = 0.50 \mathrm{~mol~kg}^{-1}$

Answer: $0.50 \mathrm{~mol} \mathrm{~kg}^{-1}$

</details>

### Q123. MHT CET 2024 (02 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

Calculate the molar mass of non volatile solute when 5 g of it is dissolved in 50 g solvent, boils at $119.6^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$.
$\left[\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{b}}=3.2 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}\right.$, boiling point of pure solvent $\left.=118^{\circ} \mathrm{C}\right]$.

**Options:**

- **(A)** $180 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(B)** $210 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(C)** $200 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(D)** $190 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $200 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The elevation in boiling point is given by $\Delta T_b = T_b - T_b^\circ$

$\Delta T_b = 119.6 - 118 = 1.6 \text{ K}$

Using the formula for elevation in boiling point:

$\Delta T_b = K_b \times m$

$\Delta T_b = K_b \times \dfrac{w_2 \times 1000}{M_2 \times w_1}$

Substituting the given values:

$1.6 = 3.2 \times \dfrac{5 \times 1000}{M_2 \times 50}$

$1.6 = 3.2 \times \dfrac{100}{M_2}$

$1.6 = \dfrac{320}{M_2}$

$M_2 = \dfrac{320}{1.6} = 200 \text{ g mol}^{-1}$

Answer: $200 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$

</details>

### Q124. MHT CET 2024 (02 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Calculate van't Hoff factor of 0.2 m aqueous solution of an electrolyte if it freezes at -0.7 $^\circ$C $\left[\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{f}}=1.86 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}\right]$

**Options:**

- **(A)** 1.304
- **(B)** 1.123
- **(C)** 1.432
- **(D)** 1.882

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) 1.882**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given, molality $m = 0.2$ m

Freezing point of the solution $T_f = -0.7$ $^{\circ}$C

Depression in freezing point $\Delta T_f = 0 - (-0.7) = 0.7$ $^{\circ}$C

Cryoscopic constant $K_f = 1.86$ K kg mol$^{-1}$

Using the formula for depression in freezing point:

$\Delta T_f = i \times K_f \times m$

Substituting the given values:

$0.7 = i \times 1.86 \times 0.2$

$i = \dfrac{0.7}{1.86 \times 0.2}$

$i = \dfrac{0.7}{0.372}$

$i = 1.8817 \approx 1.882$

Answer: 1.882

</details>

---

### Q125. MHT CET 2024 (02 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Identify false statement form following.

**Options:**

- **(A)** The boiling point of solution containing non volatile solute is always higher than pure solvent.
- **(B)** At any temperature the vapour pressure of solution containing non volatile solute is lower than that of pure solvent.
- **(C)** The boiling point of liquid is the temperature at which its vapour pressure equals atmospheric pressure.
- **(D)** The molal elevation constant is the boiling point elevation produced by 1 molar solution.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) The molal elevation constant is the boiling point elevation produced by 1 molar solution.**

**Detailed Solution:**

The boiling point of a solution containing a non-volatile solute is always higher than that of the pure solvent due to the lowering of vapour pressure. This makes the first and second statements true.

The boiling point of a liquid is defined as the temperature at which its vapour pressure becomes equal to the external atmospheric pressure. This makes the third statement true.

The molal elevation constant ($K_b$) is defined as the elevation in boiling point produced when the molality of the solution is $1$ molal ($1$ mole of solute per kg of solvent), not $1$ molar. Therefore, the fourth statement is false.

Answer: The molal elevation constant is the boiling point elevation produced by 1 molar solution.

</details>

---

Calculate the molality of solution of non volatile solute having depression in freezing point 0.93 K and cryoscopic constant of solvent $1.86 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$.

- **(A)** $0.3 \mathrm{~mol} \mathrm{~kg}^{-1}$
- **(B)** $0.4 \mathrm{~mol} \mathrm{~kg}^{-1}$
- **(C)** $0.5 \mathrm{~mol} \mathrm{~kg}^{-1}$
- **(D)** $0.6 \mathrm{~mol} \mathrm{~kg}^{-1}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $0.5 \mathrm{~mol} \mathrm{~kg}^{-1}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The depression in freezing point is given by the formula:

$\Delta T_f = K_f \times m$

where $\Delta T_f$ is the depression in freezing point, $K_f$ is the cryoscopic constant, and $m$ is the molality of the solution.

Substituting the given values:

$0.93 = 1.86 \times m$

$m = \dfrac{0.93}{1.86}$

$m = 0.5 \text{ mol kg}^{-1}$

Answer: $0.5 \mathrm{~mol} \mathrm{~kg}^{-1}$

</details>

### Q127. MHT CET 2023 (14 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A solution of nonvolatile solute is obtained by dissolving $15 \mathrm{~g}$ in $200 \mathrm{~mL}$ water has depression in freezing point $0.75 \mathrm{~K}$. Calculate the molar mass of solute if cryoscopic constant of water is $1.86 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$.

**Options:**

- **(A)** $160 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(B)** $172 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(C)** $186 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(D)** $198 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $186 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} \mathrm{M}_2 & =\frac{1000 \mathrm{~K}_{\mathrm{f}} \mathrm{W}_2}{\Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{f}} \mathrm{W}_1} \\ & =\frac{1000 \times 1.86 \times 15}{0.75 \times 200} \\ & =186 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q129. MHT CET 2023 (14 May Shift 1)

Calculate the depression in freezing point of solution when $4 \mathrm{~g}$ nonvolatile solute of molar mass $126 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$ dissolved in $80 \mathrm{~mL}$ water. $\left[\right.$ Cryoscopic constant of water $\left.=1.86 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}\right]$

**Options:**

- **(B)** $0.74 \mathrm{~K}$
- **(C)** $0.86 \mathrm{~K}$
- **(D)** $0.96 \mathrm{~K}$

<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

### Q130. MHT CET 2023 (14 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Calculate the relative lowering of vapour pressure if the vapour pressure of benzene and vapour pressure of solution of non-volatile solute in benzene are $640 \mathrm{mmHg}$ and $590 \mathrm{mmHg}$ respectively at same temperature.

**Options:**

- **(A)** $0.078$
- **(B)** $0.175$
- **(C)** $0.061$
- **(D)** $0.092$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $0.078$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Relative lowering of vapour pressure
$$
\begin{aligned}
& =\frac{\Delta \mathrm{P}}{\mathrm{P}_1^0}=\frac{\mathrm{P}_1^0-\mathrm{P}_1}{\mathrm{P}_1^0} \\
& =\frac{640-590}{640}=0.078
\end{aligned}
$$

</details>

### Q131. MHT CET 2023 (13 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

If $0.01 \mathrm{~m}$ aqueous solution of an electrolyte freezes at $-0.056{ }^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$. Calculate van't Hoff factor for an electrolyte. (Cryoscopic constant of water $=1.86 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$ )

**Options:**

- **(A)** 1.30
- **(B)** 2.33
- **(C)** 3.00
- **(D)** 4.11

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) 3.00**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{f}}=\mathrm{i} \times \mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{f}} \times \mathrm{m}$
$0.056=\mathrm{i} \times 1.86 \times 0.01$
$\therefore \quad \mathrm{i}=\frac{0.056}{1.86 \times 0.01}=3.01$
[Note: In the question, the freezing point of aqueous solution is changed from $-0.056 \mathrm{~K}$ to $-0.056^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$ to apply appropriate textual concepts.]

</details>

<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

$=\frac{7.15 \mathrm{~K}}{2.75 \mathrm{Kkg} \mathrm{mol}^{-1}}$
$=2.6 \mathrm{~mol} \mathrm{~kg}^{-1}$

### Q133. MHT CET 2023 (13 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

What is the molal elevation constant if one gram mole of a nonvolatile solute is dissolved in $1 \mathrm{~kg}$ of ethyl acetate? $\left(\Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{b}}=x \mathrm{~K}\right)$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\quad x \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(B)** $\frac{x}{2} \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(C)** $\quad 2 x \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(D)** $3 x \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\quad x \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} & \mathrm{M}_2=\frac{1000 \mathrm{~K}_{\mathrm{b}} \mathrm{W}_2}{\Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{b}} \mathrm{W}_1} \\ \therefore \quad & \mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{b}}=\frac{\mathrm{M}_2 \Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{b}} \mathrm{W}_1}{1000 \mathrm{~W}_2} \\ & \mathrm{Now}, \mathrm{W}_2=\text { one gram mole }=\mathrm{M}_2 \mathrm{~g} \\ & \mathrm{~W}_1=1 \mathrm{~kg}=1000 \mathrm{~g} \\ \therefore \quad & \mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{b}}=\frac{\mathrm{M}_2 \times x \times 1000}{1000 \times \mathrm{M}_2}=x \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q135. MHT CET 2023 (13 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Calculate van't Hoff factor of $\mathrm{K}_2 \mathrm{SO}_4$ if $0.1 \mathrm{~m}$ aqueous solution of $\mathrm{K}_2 \mathrm{SO}_4$ freezes at $-0.43{ }^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$ and cryoscopic constant of water is $1.86 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$.

- **(A)** 2.3
- **(B)** 2.7
- **(C)** 3.1
- **(D)** 3.5

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) 2.3**

**Detailed Solution:**

$$
\begin{array}{ll} 
& \Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{f}}=\mathrm{i} \mathrm{K} \mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{f}} \mathrm{m} \\
\therefore \quad & 0.43=\mathrm{i} \times 1.86 \times 0.1 \\
\therefore \quad & \mathrm{i}=\frac{0.43}{1.86 \times 0.1}=2.3
\end{array}
$$
[Note: In the question, the freezing point of aqueous solution is changed from $-0.43 \mathrm{~K}$ to $-0.43^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$ to apply appropriate textual concepts.]

</details>

### Q136. MHT CET 2023 (12 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

If $0.15 \mathrm{~m}$ aqueous solution of $\mathrm{KCI}$ freezes at $-0.51^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$, calculate van't Hoff factor of $\mathrm{KCI}$ (cryoscopic constant of water is $1.86 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$ )

- **(A)** $1.45$
- **(B)** $1.26$
- **(C)** $1.82$
- **(D)** $3.00$

<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $1.82$**

</details>

### Q137. MHT CET 2023 (12 May Shift 1)

Calculate molality of solution of a nonvolatile solute having boiling point elevation $1.89 \mathrm{~K}$ if boiling point elevation constant of solvent is $3.15 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$.

**Options:**

- **(A)** $0.4 \mathrm{~m}$
- **(B)** $0.8 \mathrm{~m}$
- **(C)** $0.6 \mathrm{~m}$
- **(D)** $0.3 \mathrm{~m}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $0.6 \mathrm{~m}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} \Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{b}} & =\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{b}} \times \mathrm{m} \\ 1.89 & =3.15 \times \mathrm{m} \\ \therefore \quad \mathrm{m} & =\frac{1.89}{3.15}=0.6 \mathrm{~mol} \mathrm{~kg}^{-1}\end{aligned}$

</details>

---

### Q138. MHT CET 2023 (12 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Calculate osmotic pressure of solution of 0.025 mole glucose in $100 \mathrm{~mL}$ water at $300 \mathrm{~K}$. $\left[\mathrm{R}=0.082 \mathrm{~atm} \mathrm{dm}^3 \mathrm{~mol}^{-1} \mathrm{~K}^{-1}\right]$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $1.54 \mathrm{~atm}$
- **(B)** $2.05 \mathrm{~atm}$
- **(C)** $6.15 \mathrm{~atm}$
- **(D)** $3.08 \mathrm{~atm}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $6.15 \mathrm{~atm}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} \pi & =\mathrm{MRT}=\frac{\mathrm{n}_2 \mathrm{RT}}{\mathrm{V}} \\ & =\frac{0.025 \mathrm{~mol} \times 0.082 \mathrm{dm}^3 \mathrm{~atm} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1} \mathrm{~K}^{-1} \times 300 \mathrm{~K}}{0.1 \mathrm{dm}^3} \\ & =6.15 \mathrm{~atm}\end{aligned}$

</details>

---

### Q139. MHT CET 2023 (12 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Which among the following solutions has minimum boiling point elevation?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $0.1 \mathrm{~m} \mathrm{NaCl}$
- **(B)** $0.2 \mathrm{~m} \mathrm{KNO}_3$
- **(C)** $0.1 \mathrm{~m} \mathrm{Na}_2 \mathrm{SO}_4$
- **(D)** $0.05 \mathrm{~m} \mathrm{CaCl}_2$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $0.05 \mathrm{~m} \mathrm{CaCl}_2$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$$
\mathrm{NaCl} \longrightarrow \mathrm{Na}^{+}+\mathrm{Cl}^{-}
$$
Total ions $=0.1+0.1=0.2$ ions
$$
\mathrm{KNO}_3 \longrightarrow \mathrm{K}^{+}+\mathrm{NO}_3^{-}
$$
Total ions $=0.2+0.2=0.4$ ions
$$
\mathrm{Na}_2 \mathrm{SO}_4 \longrightarrow 2 \mathrm{Na}^{+}+\mathrm{SO}_4^{2-}
$$
Total ions $=0.2+0.1=0.3$ ions
$$
\mathrm{CaCl}_2 \longrightarrow \mathrm{Ca}^{2+}+2 \mathrm{Cl}^{-}
$$
Total ions $=0.05+0.1=0.15$ ions $0.05 \mathrm{~m} \mathrm{CaCl}_2$ solution has minimum ions in solution, so it shows minimum boiling point elevation.

</details>

---

### Q140. MHT CET 2023 (11 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Calculate osmotic pressure of $0.2 \mathrm{M}$ aqueous $\mathrm{KCl}$ solution at $0^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$ if van't Hoff factor for $\mathrm{KCl}$ is $1.83 .\left[\mathrm{R}=0.082 \mathrm{dm}^3\right.$ atm mol$\left.{ }^{-1} \mathrm{~K}^{-1}\right]$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $8.2 \mathrm{~atm}$
- **(B)** $9.4 \mathrm{~atm}$
- **(C)** $10.6 \mathrm{~atm}$
- **(D)** $6.5 \mathrm{~atm}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $8.2 \mathrm{~atm}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} \pi & =\mathrm{iMRT} \\ & =1.83 \times 0.2 \times 0.082 \times 273 \\ & =8.2 \mathrm{~atm}\end{aligned}$

</details>

---

### Q141. MHT CET 2023 (11 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A solution of nonvolatile solute is obtained by dissolving $1.5 \mathrm{~g}$ in $30 \mathrm{~g}$ solvent has boiling point elevation $0.65 \mathrm{~K}$. Calculate the molal elevation constant if molar mass of solute is $150 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$.

**Options:**

- **(A)** $1.95 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(B)** $2.23 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(C)** $1.52 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(D)** $2.72 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $1.95 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} \mathrm{M}_2=\frac{1000 . \mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{b}} \mathrm{W}_2}{\Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{b}} \mathrm{W}_1} & \\ \mathrm{~K}_{\mathrm{b}}=\frac{\mathrm{M}_2^2 \times \Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{b}} \times \mathrm{W}_1}{1000 \times \mathrm{W}_2} & =\frac{150 \times 0.65 \times 30}{1000 \times 1.5} \\ & =1.95 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}\end{aligned}$

</details>

---

### Q142. MHT CET 2023 (11 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

If $K_b$ denote molal elevation constant of water, then boiling point of an aqueous solution containing $36 \mathrm{~g}$ glucose $($ molar mass $=180)$ per $\mathrm{dm}^3$ is:

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\left(100+\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{b}}\right)^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$
- **(B)** $\left(100+2 \mathrm{~K}_{\mathrm{b}}\right)^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$
- **(C)** $\left(100+\frac{\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{b}}}{10}\right){ }^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$
- **(D)** $\left(100+\frac{2 \mathrm{~K}_{\mathrm{b}}}{10}\right){ }^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $\left(100+\frac{2 \mathrm{~K}_{\mathrm{b}}}{10}\right){ }^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The aqueous solution contains $36 \mathrm{~g}$ glucose per $\mathrm{dm}^3$, so mass of solute $\mathrm{W}_2$ is $36 \mathrm{~g}$.

Assuming that the density of solution is $1 \mathrm{~g} / \mathrm{dm}^3$, the mass of solvent (water) is $1000 \mathrm{~g}$.
$$
\begin{aligned}
& \Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{b}}=\frac{1000 \mathrm{~K}_{\mathrm{b}} \mathrm{W}_2}{\mathrm{M}_2 \mathrm{~W}_1} \\
\Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{b}} & =\frac{1000 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~kg}^{-1} \times \mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{b}} \times 36 \mathrm{~g}}{180 \mathrm{~g} \times 1000 \mathrm{~g}} \\
\Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{b}} & =\frac{2 \mathrm{~K}_{\mathrm{b}}}{10} \\
\Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{b}} & =\mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{b}}-\mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{b}}^0 \\
& \mathrm{~T}_{\mathrm{b}}=\mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{b}}^0+\Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{b}} \\
\therefore \quad & \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{b}}=\left(100+\frac{2 \mathrm{~K}_{\mathrm{b}}}{10}\right)^{\circ} \mathrm{C}
\end{aligned}
$$

</details>

---

### Q143. MHT CET 2023 (10 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

What is osmotic pressure of solution of $1.7 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{CaCl}_2$ in $1.25 \mathrm{dm}^3$ water at $300 \mathrm{~K}$ if van't Hoff factor and molar mass of $\mathrm{CaCl}_2$, are 2.47 and $111 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$ respectively?
$\left[\mathrm{R}=0.082 \mathrm{dm}^3 \text { atm mol }{ }^{-1} \mathrm{~K}^{-1}\right]$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $0.625 \mathrm{~atm}$
- **(B)** $0.744 \mathrm{~atm}$
- **(C)** $0.827 \mathrm{~atm}$
- **(D)** $0.936 \mathrm{~atm}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $0.744 \mathrm{~atm}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\pi=\mathrm{iMRT}=\frac{\mathrm{i} \times \mathrm{W}_2 \mathrm{RT}}{\mathrm{M}_2 \mathrm{~V}}$
$\begin{aligned} & \pi=\frac{2.47 \times 1.7 \mathrm{~g} \times 0.082 \mathrm{dm}^3 \mathrm{~atm} \mathrm{~mol} \mathrm{~K}^{-1} \times 300 \mathrm{~K}}{111 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1} \times 1.25 \mathrm{dm}^3} \\ & =0.744 \mathrm{~atm} \\ & \end{aligned}$

</details>

---

### Q144. MHT CET 2023 (10 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Weak acid HX has dissociation constant $1 \times 10^{-5}$. Calculate the percent dissociation in its $0.1 \mathrm{M}$ solution.

**Options:**

- **(A)** $2.2 \%$
- **(B)** $3.5 \%$
- **(C)** $4.2 \%$
- **(D)** $1.0 \%$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $1.0 \%$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} & \mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{a}}=1 \times 10^{-5}, \mathrm{c}=0.1 \mathrm{M} \\ \mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{a}} & =\alpha^2 \mathrm{c} \\ \therefore \quad \alpha & =\sqrt{\frac{\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{a}}}{\mathrm{c}}}=\sqrt{\frac{1 \times 10^{-5}}{0.1}}=\sqrt{1 \times 10^{-4}}=0.01\end{aligned}$
$\begin{aligned} & \alpha=\frac{\text { Percent dissociation }}{100} \\ & \text { Percent dissociation }=\alpha \times 100 \\ & =0.01 \times 100=1.0 \% \\ & \end{aligned}$

</details>

---

Moles of particles in \\

### Q146. MHT CET 2023 (10 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Acetic acid dissociated to $1.20 \%$ in its $0.01 \mathrm{M}$ solution. What is the value of its dissociation constant?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $2.20 \times 10^{-2}$
- **(B)** $1.60 \times 10^{-4}$
- **(C)** $1.44 \times 10^{-6}$
- **(D)** $2.40 \times 10^{-4}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $1.44 \times 10^{-6}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Percent dissociation $=1.20 \%$
Degree of dissociation $(\alpha)=0.012$
For a weak monobasic acid, $\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{a}}=\alpha^2 \mathrm{c}=(0.012)^2 \times 0.01=1.44 \times 10^{-6}$

</details>

### Q147. MHT CET 2023 (10 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Find the depression in freezing point of solution when 3.2 gram non volatile solute with molar mass $128 \mathrm{gram} \mathrm{mol}^{-1}$ is dissolved in 80 gram solvent if cryoscopic constant of solvent is $4.8 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$.

**Options:**

- **(A)** $3.0 \mathrm{~K}$
- **(B)** $1.5 \mathrm{~K}$
- **(C)** $2.0 \mathrm{~K}$
- **(D)** $2.5 \mathrm{~K}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $1.5 \mathrm{~K}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} \Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{f}} & =\frac{1000 \mathrm{~K}_{\mathrm{f}} \mathrm{W}_2}{\mathrm{M}_2 \mathrm{~W}_1} \\ & =\frac{1000 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~kg}^{-1} \times 4.8 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1} \times 3.2 \mathrm{~g}}{128 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1} \times 80 \mathrm{~g}} \\ & =1.5 \mathrm{~K}\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q149. MHT CET 2023 (09 May Shift 2)

What is the concentration of $\left[\mathrm{H}_3 \mathrm{O}^{+}\right]$ion in $\mathrm{mol} \mathrm{L}^{-1}$ of $0.001 \mathrm{M}$ acetic acid $(\alpha=0.134)$ ?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $1.34 \times 10^{-4}$

- **(C)** $1.80 \times 10^{-4}$
- **(D)** $1.70 \times 10^{-4}$

<details>

**Detailed Solution:**

### Q150. MHT CET 2023 (09 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A solution of nonvolatile solute is obtained by dissolving $1 \mathrm{~g}$ in $100 \mathrm{~g}$ solvent, decreases its freezing point by $0.3 \mathrm{~K}$. Calculate cryoscopic constant of solvent if molar mass of solute is $60 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$.

**Options:**

- **(A)** $1.0 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(B)** $1.4 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(C)** $2.4 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(D)** $1.8 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $1.8 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} \Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{f}} & =\frac{1000 \mathrm{~K}_{\mathrm{f}} \mathrm{W}_2}{\mathrm{M}_2 \mathrm{~W}_1} \\ \therefore \quad \mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{f}} & =\frac{\Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{f}} \mathrm{M}_2 \mathrm{~W}_1}{1000 \mathrm{~W}_2} \\ & =\frac{0.3 \mathrm{~K} \times 60 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1} \times 100 \mathrm{~g}}{1000 \times 1 \mathrm{~g}} \\ & =1.8 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q151. MHT CET 2023 (09 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A solution of $8 \mathrm{~g}$ of certain organic compound in $2 \mathrm{dm}^3$ water develops osmotic pressure $0.6 \mathrm{~atm}$ at $300 \mathrm{~K}$. Calculate the molar mass of compound. $\left[\mathrm{R}=0.082 \mathrm{~atm} \mathrm{dm}^3 \mathrm{~K}^{-1} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}\right]$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $148 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(B)** $164 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(C)** $\quad 172 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(D)** $180 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $164 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} \pi & =\frac{\mathrm{W}_2 \mathrm{RT}}{\mathrm{M}_2 \mathrm{~V}} \\ \therefore \quad \mathrm{M}_2 & =\frac{\mathrm{W}_2 \mathrm{RT}}{\pi \mathrm{V}} \\ \therefore \quad \mathrm{M}_2 & =\frac{8 \mathrm{~g} \times 0.082 \mathrm{dm}^3 \mathrm{~atm} \mathrm{~K}^{-1} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1} \times 300 \mathrm{~K}}{0.6 \mathrm{~atm} \times 2 \mathrm{dm}^3} \\ & =164 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}\end{aligned}$

</details>

---

### Q152. MHT CET 2023 (09 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A solution of nonvolatile solute is obtained by dissolving $3.5 \mathrm{~g}$ in $100 \mathrm{~g}$ solvent has boiling point elevation $0.35 \mathrm{~K}$. Calculate the molar mass of solute.
$\left(\right.$ Molal elevation constant $\left.=2.5 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}\right)$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $270 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(B)** $260 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(C)** $250 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(D)** $240 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $250 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\mathrm{M}_2=\frac{\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{b}} \times \mathrm{W}_2 \times 1000}{\Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{b}} \times \mathrm{W}_1}=\frac{2.5 \times 3.5 \times 1000}{0.35 \times 100}=250 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$

</details>

---

### Q153. MHT CET 2023 (09 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

$0.2 \mathrm{M}$ aqueous solution of glucose has osmotic pressure $4.9 \mathrm{~atm}$ at $300 \mathrm{~K}$. What is the concentration of glucose if it has osmotic pressure $1.5 \mathrm{~atm}$ at same temperature?

**Options:**

- **(A)** 0.03 M
- **(B)** 0.04 M
- **(C)** 0.05 M
- **(D)** 0.06 M

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) 0.06 M**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} & \pi=\mathrm{M} \times \mathrm{R} \times \mathrm{T} \\ \therefore \quad & \mathrm{M}=\frac{\pi}{\mathrm{RT}}=\frac{1.5}{0.082 \times 300}=0.06 \mathrm{M}\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q154. MHT CET 2022 (11 Aug Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

What is the percentage dissociation of $0.1 \mathrm{M}$ acetic acid?
$\left(\mathrm{Ka}=10^{-5}\right)$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $1 \%$
- **(B)** $10 \%$
- **(C)** $0.1 \%$
- **(D)** $0.01 \%$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $1 \%$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} & \mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{a}}=\propto^2 \mathrm{C} \\ & 10^{-5}=\propto^2 \times 0.1 \\ & \propto=0.01 \text { or } 1 \%\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q157. MHT CET 2022 (10 Aug Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

What is the $\mathrm{pH}$ of $2 \times 10^{-3} \mathrm{M}$ solution of monoacidic weak base if it ionizes to the extent of $5 \%$

**Options:**

- **(A)** 10
- **(B)** 6
- **(C)** 4
- **(D)** 14

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) 4**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} & {\left[\mathrm{H}^{+}\right]=\alpha \mathrm{c}} \\ & \mathrm{C}=2 \times 10^{-3} \mathrm{M} \\ & \alpha=0.05 \\ & {\left[\mathrm{H}^{+}\right]=2 \times 10^{-3} \times 0.05} \\ & =1 \times 10^{-4} \mathrm{M} \\ & \mathrm{pH}=4\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q158. MHT CET 2022 (10 Aug Shift 2)

$\mathrm{W}_{\mathrm{B}}=64 \mathrm{~g}$

</details>

### Q159. MHT CET 2022 (10 Aug Shift 1)

</details>

### Q161. MHT CET 2022 (10 Aug Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Calculate the cryoscopic constant when 0.8 gram nonvolatile solute with molar mass $64 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$ dissolved in 43 gram solvent lowers the freezing point by $0.34 \mathrm{~K}$.

**Options:**

- **(A)** $2.5 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(B)** $2.0 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(C)** $0.85 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(D)** $1.17 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} & \Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{f}}=\mathrm{k}_{\mathrm{f}} \mathrm{m} \\ & 0.34=\mathrm{k}_{\mathrm{f}} \times \frac{0.8}{64} \times \frac{1000}{43} \\ & \mathrm{k}_{\mathrm{f}}=1.169 \sim 1.17\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q163. MHT CET 2022 (08 Aug Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Calculate the amount of solute dissolved in 612 grams of water at $30^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$ if molar mass of solute is $342 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$ (Relative vapour pressure lowering is 0.025 and molar mass of water $18 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$ )

**Options:**

- **(A)** 142.5 gram
- **(B)** 270.6 gram
- **(C)** 240.2 gram
- **(D)** 290.7 gram

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) 290.7 gram**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\frac{\Delta P}{P_A^{\circ}}=x_B \simeq \frac{n_B}{n_A}$
$\frac{W_B}{342} \times \frac{18}{612}=0.025$
$W_B=290.7$

</details>

### Q164. MHT CET 2022 (08 Aug Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

What is the molar mass of solute if a solution is prepared by dissolving 4.5 gram solute in $3 \mathrm{dm}^3$ water having osmotic pressure $0.25 \mathrm{~atm}$ at $300 \mathrm{~K}$ ?
$\left(\mathrm{R}=0.0821 \mathrm{dm}^3 \mathrm{~atm} \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~K}^{-1} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}\right)$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $148 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(B)** $160 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(C)** $172 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(D)** $136 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$

<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

$0.25=\frac{4.5}{\mathrm{M}_{\mathrm{B}}} \times \frac{1000}{3000} \times 0.0821 \times 300$
$\mathrm{M}_{\mathrm{B}}=147.78 \mathrm{~g} / \mathrm{mol}$

</details>

### Q165. MHT CET 2022 (08 Aug Shift 1)

$\begin{aligned}

### Q166. MHT CET 2022 (08 Aug Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

What is vapour pressure of a solution containing $1.8 g$ glucose in 16. $2 \mathrm{~g}$ of water if vapour pressure of water is $32 \mathrm{~mm} \mathrm{Hg}$ ?

**Options:**

- **(A)** 22. $2 \mathrm{~mm} \mathrm{Hg}$
- **(B)** 26. $6 \mathrm{~mm} \mathrm{Hg}$
- **(C)** 24. $6 \mathrm{~mm} \mathrm{Hg}$
- **(D)** 31. $7 \mathrm{~mm} \mathrm{Hg}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) 31. $7 \mathrm{~mm} \mathrm{Hg}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} & \frac{P_A^{\circ}-P_A}{P_A^{\circ}}=x_B \simeq \frac{n_B}{n_A} \\ & \frac{32-P_A}{32}=\frac{1.8}{180} \times \frac{18}{16.2} \\ & =0.0111 \\ & P_A=32-0.3555 \\ & =31.7 \mathrm{~mm} \\ & \end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q168. MHT CET 2022 (07 Aug Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Find the molar mass of solute when 2 gram dissolved in 60 gram benzene at $30^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$ and relative lowering of vapour pressure is 0.06 . (Molar mass of benzene is $78 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}$ )

**Options:**

- **(A)** 17.4 gram $\mathrm{mol}^{-1}$
- **(B)** 35.2 gram mol-1
- **(C)** 43.3 gram $\mathrm{mol}^{-1}$
- **(D)** 24.2 gram mol-1

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) 43.3 gram $\mathrm{mol}^{-1}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\frac{\Delta \mathrm{P}}{\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{A}}^{\circ}}=\frac{\mathrm{W}_{\mathrm{B}}}{\mathrm{M}_{\mathrm{B}}} \times \frac{\mathrm{M}_{\mathrm{A}}}{\mathrm{W}_{\mathrm{A}}}$
$0.06=\frac{2}{M_B} \times \frac{78}{60}$
$M_B=\frac{78}{30 \times 0.06}=43.3 \mathrm{~g} / \mathrm{mol}$

</details>
