### Q175. MHT CET 2022 (06 Aug Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Calculate the molar mass of solute when 1.5 gram nonvolatile solute dissolved in $100 \mathrm{~mL}$ solvent having density $0.8 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mL}^{-1}$ lowers its freezing point by $0.75 \mathrm{~K}$.
(Freezing point depression constant for solvent is $5 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$ )

**Options:**

- **(A)** $125 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(B)** $110 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(C)** $100 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(D)** $75 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $125 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} & \Delta T_f=k_f \cdot \frac{W_B}{M_B} \times \frac{1000}{W_A} \\ & 0.75=5 \times \frac{1.5}{M_B} \times \frac{1000}{80} \\ & M_B=125 \mathrm{~g} / \mathrm{mol}\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q180. MHT CET 2022 (05 Aug Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

What is the molar mass of solute when 2.3 gram non-volatile solute dissolved in 46 gram benzene at $30^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$ ?
(Relative lowering of vapour pressure is 0.06 and molar mass of benzene is 78 gram mol$^{-1}$ )

**Options:**

- **(A)** 65 gram mol${ }^{-1}$
- **(B)** $80 \mathrm{gram} \mathrm{mol}^{-1}$
- **(C)** 72 gram mol${ }^{-1}$
- **(D)** $48 \mathrm{gram} \mathrm{mol}^{-1}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) 65 gram mol${ }^{-1}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} & \frac{\Delta \mathrm{P}}{\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{A}}^0}=\frac{\mathrm{n}_{\mathrm{B}}}{\mathrm{n}_{\mathrm{A}}}=\frac{\mathrm{W}_{\mathrm{B}}}{\mathrm{M}_{\mathrm{B}}} \times \frac{\mathrm{M}_{\mathrm{A}}}{\mathrm{W}_{\mathrm{A}}} \\ & 0.06=\frac{2.3}{\mathrm{M}_{\mathrm{B}}} \times \frac{78}{46}\end{aligned}$
$M_B=65$

</details>

### Q181. MHT CET 2021 (24 Sep Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The solution containing $3 \mathrm{~g}$ urea (molar mass 60 ) per $\mathrm{dm}^3$ of water and another solution containing $4.5 \mathrm{~g}$ of solute A per $\mathrm{dm}^3$ boils at same temperature, then what is molar mass of A?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $54 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(B)** $180 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(C)** $120 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(D)** $90 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $90 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{b}}=\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{b}} \cdot \mathrm{m}$
$\text { molality }(\mathrm{m})=\frac{\text { moles of solute }}{\text { mass of water }(\mathrm{kg})}$
1L $\mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}=1 \mathrm{dm}^3 \mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}=1 \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}$
Density of water $=1 \mathrm{~kg} / \mathrm{L}$
Boiling points are same for two solutions,
$\begin{aligned}
& \Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{b}_1}=\Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{b}_2} \\
& \mathrm{~m}_1=\mathrm{m}_2
\end{aligned}$
$\begin{aligned}
& \frac{3 / 60}{1}=\frac{4.5 / \mathrm{M}}{1} \\
& \mathrm{M}=\frac{60 \times 4.5}{3} \\
& =90 \mathrm{~g} / \mathrm{mol}
\end{aligned}$

</details>

---

### Q182. MHT CET 2021 (24 Sep Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

According to Raoult's law mole fraction of solute in solution is given by formula

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{\Delta \mathrm{P}}{\mathrm{P}_1^0}$
- **(B)** $\frac{\mathrm{P}_1^0}{\mathrm{P}_1}$
- **(C)** $\frac{\mathrm{P}_1^0}{\Delta \mathrm{P}}$
- **(D)** $\frac{\mathrm{P}_1}{\mathrm{P}_1^0}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\frac{\Delta \mathrm{P}}{\mathrm{P}_1^0}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$P_1=$ Vapour pressure of solution
$\mathrm{P}_1^0=$ Vapour pressure of pure solvent
Mole fraction of solute $=\frac{\mathrm{P}_1^0-\mathrm{P}_1}{\mathrm{P}_1^0}=\frac{\Delta \mathrm{P}}{\mathrm{P}_1^0}$

</details>

---

### Q183. MHT CET 2021 (24 Sep Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Vapour pressure of solution and of pure solvent are $\mathrm{P}_1$ and $\mathrm{P}_1{ }^0$ respectively.
If $\frac{P_1}{P_1^0}$ is 0.15 , find the mole fraction of solute.

**Options:**

- **(A)** 0.66
- **(B)** 0.85
- **(C)** 0.15
- **(D)** 0.33

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) 0.85**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} & \frac{\mathrm{P}_1^0-\mathrm{P}_1}{\mathrm{P}_1^0}=\text { mole fraction of solute } \\ & 1-\frac{\mathrm{P}_1}{\mathrm{P}_1^0}=\mathrm{x}_{\text {solute }} \\ & \mathrm{x}_{\text {solute }}=1-0.15=0.85\end{aligned}$

</details>

---

### Q184. MHT CET 2021 (24 Sep Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The solution containing $6 \mathrm{~g}$ urea (molar mass 60 ) per $\mathrm{dm}^3$ of water and another solution containing $9 \mathrm{~g}$ of solute $\mathrm{A}$ per $\mathrm{dm}^3$ water freezes at same temperature. What is molar mass of A?

**Options:**

- **(A)** 90
- **(B)** 180
- **(C)** 54
- **(D)** 120

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) 90**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} & \left(\Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{i}}\right)_1=\left(\Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{f}}\right)_2 \\ & \mathrm{~m}_1=\mathrm{m}_2 \\ & \mathrm{~m}=\text { molality, } 1 \mathrm{dm}^3=1 \mathrm{~L}=1000 \mathrm{ml} \mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O} \\ & 1000 \mathrm{ml} \mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}=1000 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O} \\ & \mathrm{m}=\frac{\mathrm{mol} / \mathrm{M}}{\text { Mass of } \mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}} \\ & \frac{6 / 60}{1}=\frac{9 / \mathrm{M}_{\mathrm{A}}}{1} \\ & \mathrm{M}_{\mathrm{A}}=\frac{60 \times 9}{6}=90\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q185. MHT CET 2021 (23 Sep Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

What is the freezing point of 1 molal aqueous solution of a non-volatile solute?
$$
\left(\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{f}}=1.86 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}\right)\left(\mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{f}}^{\circ} \text { for water }=0^{\circ} \mathrm{C}\right)
$$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $-0.93^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$
- **(B)** $-2.43^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$
- **(C)** $-3.72^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$
- **(D)** $-1.86^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $-1.86^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} & \Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{f}}=\mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{f}}^{\circ}-\mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{f}}=\mathrm{k}_{\mathrm{f}} \cdot \mathrm{m} \\ & 0^{\circ} \mathrm{C}-\mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{f}}=1.86 \times 1 \\ & \mathrm{~T}_{\mathrm{f}}=-1.86^{\circ} \mathrm{C}\end{aligned}$

</details>

---

### Q186. MHT CET 2021 (23 Sep Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

What is vapour pressure of solution containing $1.8 \mathrm{~g}$ glucose in $16.2 \mathrm{~g}$ water?
$$
\left(\mathrm{P}_1^0=24 \mathrm{~mm} \mathrm{Hg} \text { and Molar mas of gloucose }=180 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}\right)
$$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $18.1 \mathrm{~mm} \mathrm{Hg}$
- **(B)** $15.7 \mathrm{~mm} \mathrm{Hg}$
- **(C)** $12.4 \mathrm{~mm} \mathrm{Hg}$
- **(D)** $23.8 \mathrm{~mm} \mathrm{Hg}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $23.8 \mathrm{~mm} \mathrm{Hg}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} & \frac{\mathrm{P}^0-\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{s}}}{\mathrm{P}^0}=\mathrm{X}_{\text {solute }}=\frac{\mathrm{n}_{\text {solute }}}{\mathrm{n}_{\text {solute }}+\mathrm{n}_{\text {solvent }}} \\ & \mathrm{n}_{\text {glucose }}=\frac{1.8}{180}=0.01 \\ & \mathrm{n}_{\text {water }}=\frac{16.2}{18}=0.9 \\ & \frac{24-\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{s}}}{24}=\frac{0.01}{0.01+0.9}=\frac{1}{91} \\ & \left(24-\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{s}}\right) 91=24 \\ & \mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{s}}=24-\frac{24}{91}=23.8 \mathrm{~mm} \mathrm{Hg}\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q187. MHT CET 2021 (23 Sep Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Calculate osmotic pressure exerted by a solution containing $0.822 \mathrm{~g}$ of solute in $300 \mathrm{~mL}$ of water at $300 \mathrm{~K}$.
(Molar mass of solute $=340 \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}, \mathrm{R}=0.0821 \mathrm{~L} \mathrm{~atm} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1} \mathrm{~K}^{-1}$ )

**Options:**

- **(A)** 0.5 atm
- **(B)** 0.2 atm
- **(C)** 0.1 atm
- **(D)** 0.4 atm

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) 0.2 atm**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} & \pi=\mathrm{CRT} \\ & \mathrm{C}=\frac{\text { moles of solute }}{\text { volumeof solution }(\mathrm{ml})} \times 1000 \\ & =\frac{0.822 / 340}{300} \times 1000 \\ & =0.008 \\ & \pi=0.008 \times 0.0821 \times 300 \\ & \pi=0.2 \mathrm{~atm}\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q188. MHT CET 2021 (22 Sep Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Which of the following formulae is used to obtain depression in freezing point?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{f}}=\frac{\mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{f}}^{\circ}}{\mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{f}}}$
- **(B)** $\Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{f}}=\mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{f}}^{\circ}-\mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{f}}$
- **(C)** $\Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{f}}=\mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{f}}-\mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{f}}^{\circ}$
- **(D)** $\Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{f}}=\frac{\mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{f}}}{\mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{f}}^{\circ}}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $\Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{f}}=\mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{f}}^{\circ}-\mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{f}}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$$
\Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{f}}=\mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{f}}^{\circ}-\mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{f}}
$$
Where, $\mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{f}}^{\circ}=$ freezing point of pure solvent $T_f=$ freezing point of solution.

</details>

### Q189. MHT CET 2021 (22 Sep Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

$5 \mathrm{~g}$ sucrose (molar mass $=342$ ) is dissolved in $100 \mathrm{~g}$ of solvent, decreases the freezing point by $2.15 \mathrm{~K}$. What is cryoscopic constant of solvent?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $14.7 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(B)** $2.15 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(C)** $4.30 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(D)** $7.35 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $14.7 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} & \Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{f}}=\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{f} \mathrm{m}} \\ & \mathrm{m}=\frac{\text { moles of solute }}{\text { massof solvent }} \times 1000 \\ & \mathrm{~m}=\frac{5 / 342}{100} \times 1000=\frac{50}{342} \\ & \Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{f}}=\mathrm{kf} \cdot \mathrm{m} \\ & 2.15=\mathrm{kf} \cdot \frac{50}{342} \\ & \mathrm{kf}=\frac{2.15 \times 342}{50}=14.7 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \cdot \mathrm{mol}^{-1}\end{aligned}$

</details>

---

### Q190. MHT CET 2021 (22 Sep Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

If $6 \mathrm{~g}$ of solute dissolved in $100 \mathrm{~g}$ of water lowers the freezing point by $0.93 \mathrm{~K}$. What is molar mass of solute?
$$
\left(\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{f}}=1.86 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}\right)
$$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $120 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(B)** $60 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(C)** $90 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(D)** $180 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $120 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} & \mathrm{m}=\frac{\text { moles of solute }}{\text { mass of solvent }} \times 1000 \\ & =\frac{6 / \mathrm{M}}{100} \times 1000=\frac{60}{\mathrm{M}} \\ & \Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{f}}=\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{f}} \cdot \mathrm{m} \\ & 0.93=1.86 \times \frac{60}{\mathrm{M}} \Rightarrow \mathrm{M}=\frac{1.86 \times 60}{0.93}=120 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}\end{aligned}$

</details>

---

### Q191. MHT CET 2021 (22 Sep Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Molal depression constant for a liquid is $2.77^{\circ} \mathrm{C} \mathrm{kg} \mathrm{mol}^{-1}$, in Kelvin scale it's value is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $275.77 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(B)** $271.77 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(C)** $2.77 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(D)** $27.7 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$

<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Detailed Solution:**

$\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{f}}=\frac{\Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{f}}}{\mathrm{m}}$ unit of $\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{f}} \Rightarrow{ }^{\circ} \mathrm{C} \mathrm{kg} \cdot \mathrm{mol}^{-1}$
Or K kg. $\mathrm{mol}^{-1}$
Temperature difference will be same in ${ }^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$ and $\mathrm{K}$.
$\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{f}}=2.77 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \cdot \mathrm{mol}^{-1}$

### Q192. MHT CET 2021 (22 Sep Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The vapour pressure of a solvent decrease by $2.5 \mathrm{~mm} \mathrm{Hg}$ by adding a solute. What is the mole fraction of solute? (Vapour pressure of pure solvent is $250 \mathrm{~mm} \mathrm{Hg}$ )

**Options:**

- **(A)** $0.88$
- **(B)** $0.01$
- **(C)** $0.1$
- **(D)** $0.99$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $0.01$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} & \frac{\mathrm{P}^{\circ}-\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{S}}}{\mathrm{P}^0}=\mathrm{X}_{\text {solute }} \\ & \mathrm{P}^{\circ}-\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{S}}=2.5 \mathrm{~mm}, \mathrm{P}^{\circ}=250 \mathrm{~mm} \\ & \frac{2.5}{250}=\mathrm{X}_{\text {solute }} \\ & \mathrm{X}_{\text {solute }}=0.01\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q193. MHT CET 2021 (21 Sep Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

What is the boiling point of 0.5 molal aqueous solution of sucrose is 0.1 molal aqueous solution of glucose boils at

**Options:**

- **(A)** $100.32^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$
- **(B)** $100.80^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$
- **(C)** $100.16^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$
- **(D)** $100.62^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $100.80^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} & \Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{b}}=\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{b}} \cdot \mathrm{m} \\ & \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{b}}-\mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{b}}^{\circ}=\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{b}} \cdot \mathrm{m} \\ & \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{b}}-100^{\circ} \mathrm{C}=\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{b}} \times 0.5 \\ & 100.16-100^{\circ} \mathrm{C}=\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{b}} \times 0.1 \\ & \left.\mathrm{~K}_{\mathrm{b}}=\frac{0.16}{0.1}=1.6 \text { (From equation } 2\right) \\ & \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{b}}-100^{\circ} \mathrm{C}=1.6 \times 0.5 \\ & \mathrm{~T}_{\mathrm{b}}=100+0.80=100.80^{\circ} \mathrm{C}\end{aligned}$

</details>

---

### Q194. MHT CET 2021 (21 Sep Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

What is vapour pressure of a solution when $2 \mathrm{~mol}$ of a non-volatile solute are dissolved in $20 \mathrm{~mol}$ of water? $\left(\mathrm{P}_1^{\circ}=32 \mathrm{~mm} \mathrm{Hg}\right)$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $29.1 \mathrm{~mm} \mathrm{Hg}$
- **(B)** $12 \mathrm{~mm} \mathrm{Hg}$
- **(C)** $6 \mathrm{~mm} \mathrm{Hg}$
- **(D)** $9 \mathrm{~mm} \mathrm{Hg}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $29.1 \mathrm{~mm} \mathrm{Hg}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} & \frac{\mathrm{P}^{\circ}-\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{s}}}{\mathrm{P}^{\circ}}=\mathrm{X}_{\text {solute }}=\frac{\mathrm{n}_{\text {solute }}}{\mathrm{n}_{\text {solute }}+\mathrm{n}_{\text {solvent }}} \\ & \frac{32-\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{s}}}{32}=\frac{2}{2+20} \\ & 32-\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{s}}=\frac{2}{22} \times 32=2.90 \\ & \mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{s}}=32-2.90 \\ & =29.1 \mathrm{~mm} \mathrm{Hg}\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q195. MHT CET 2021 (21 Sep Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

What is boiling point of a decimolal aqueous solution of glucose if molal elevation constant for water is $0.52^{\circ} \mathrm{C} \mathrm{kg} \mathrm{mol}^{-1}$ ?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $101.52^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$
- **(B)** $99.95^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$
- **(C)** $99.48^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$
- **(D)** $100.052^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $100.052^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$$
\begin{aligned}
& \Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{b}}=\mathrm{k}_{\mathrm{b}} \cdot \mathrm{m} \\
& \Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{b}}=0.52 \times 0.1, \mathrm{~m}=0.1 \text { (decimolal solution) } \\
& =0.052^{\circ} \mathrm{C} \\
& \Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{b}}=\mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{b}}-\mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{b}}^{\circ} \quad\left(\mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{b}}^{\circ}=\right.\text { Boiling point of pure water) } \\
& 0.052^{\circ} \mathrm{C}=\mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{b}}-100^{\circ} \mathrm{C} \\
& \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{b}}=100.052^{\circ} \mathrm{C}
\end{aligned}
$$

</details>

### Q196. MHT CET 2021 (20 Sep Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Which of the following solutions does not flow in either direction, when separated by semipermeable membrane? (Molar mass: glucose $=180$, urea $=60$ )

**Options:**

- **(A)** $18 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{urea} \mathrm{dm}^{-3}$
- **(B)** $6 \mathrm{~g}$ urea $\mathrm{dm}^{-3}$ and $36 \mathrm{~g}$ glucose $\mathrm{dm}^{-3}$
- **(C)** $6 \mathrm{~g}$ urea dm${ }^{-3}$ and $24 \mathrm{~g}$ glucose $\mathrm{dm}^{-3}$
- **(D)** $12 \mathrm{~g}^{\text {urea dm}}{ }^{-3}$ and $36 \mathrm{~g}$ glucose $\mathrm{dm}^{-3}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $12 \mathrm{~g}^{\text {urea dm}}{ }^{-3}$ and $36 \mathrm{~g}$ glucose $\mathrm{dm}^{-3}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$$
\begin{aligned}
& \mathrm{n}_{\text {urea }}=\frac{12}{60}=0.2 \text { and } \mathrm{n}_{\text {glucose }}=\frac{36}{180}=0.2 \\
& \text { Now, } \mathrm{n}_{\text {urea }}=\mathrm{n}_{\text {glucose }} \\
& \therefore \pi_{\text {urea }}=\pi_{\text {glucose }}
\end{aligned}
$$
Hence, if these solutions are separated by a semipermeable membrane, there is no flow of solvent in either direction.

</details>

### Q197. MHT CET 2021 (20 Sep Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A solution of $6 \mathrm{~g}$ of solute in $100 \mathrm{~g}$ of water boils at $100.52^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$. The molal elevation constant of water is $0.52 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$. What is molar mass of solute?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $60 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(B)** $120 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(C)** $90 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(D)** $180 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $60 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$$
\begin{aligned}
& \mathrm{W}_2=6 \mathrm{~g}, \mathrm{~W}_1=100 \mathrm{~g}, \mathrm{~K}_{\mathrm{b}}=0.52 \mathrm{Kg} \mathrm{mol}^{-1}, \mathrm{~T}_{\mathrm{b}} 100.52^{\circ} \mathrm{C} \\
& \therefore \Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{b}}=\mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{b}}-\mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{b}}^{\circ}=(100.52+273)-(100+273) \\
& =0.52 \mathrm{k}
\end{aligned}
$$
Now, $\mathrm{M}_2=\frac{1000 \cdot \mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{b}} \cdot \mathrm{W}_2}{\Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{b}} \cdot \mathrm{W}_1}=\frac{1000 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~kg}^{-1} \times 0.52 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1} \times 6 \mathrm{~g}}{0.52 \mathrm{~K} \times 100 \mathrm{~g}}$
$$
\therefore \mathrm{M}_2=60 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}
$$

</details>

---

### Q198. MHT CET 2021 (20 Sep Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

What is cryoscopic constant of water if $5 \mathrm{~g}$ of glucose in $100 \mathrm{~g}$ of water has depression in freezing point $2.15 \mathrm{~K}$ ? (Molar mass of glucose $=180$ )

**Options:**

- **(A)** $7.74 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(B)** $0.52 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(C)** $1.32 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(D)** $3.86 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $7.74 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} & \Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{f}}=\mathrm{kf} \cdot \mathrm{m} \\ & \mathrm{m}(\text { molality })=\frac{\text { moles of glucose }}{\text { massof water }(\mathrm{kg})} \\ & \mathrm{m}=\frac{5 / 180}{0.1 \mathrm{~kg}}=\frac{5}{18} \mathrm{~mol} / \mathrm{kg} \\ & \Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{f}}=2.15 \mathrm{~K} \\ & 2.15=\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{f}} \cdot \frac{5}{18} \\ & \mathrm{~K}_{\mathrm{f}}=7.74 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q199. MHT CET 2021 (20 Sep Shift 1)

Which of the following statements is correct for boiling point of a liquid?

- **(A)** Temperature at which a liquid boil at any pressure
- **(B)** Temperature at which solid is in equilibrium with its liquid
- **(C)** Temperature at which vapour pressure equals the applied pressure

The boiling point of a liquid is that temperature at which its vapour pressure becomes equal to the applied pressure or external pressure.

### Q201. MHT CET 2020 (20 Oct Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Which of the following formula correctly gives the value of ebuillioscopic constant?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{\mathrm{W}_{1} \times 1000}{\Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{b}} \times \mathrm{W}_{2} \times \mathrm{M}_{2}}$
- **(B)** $\frac{\mathrm{W}_{2} \times 1000}{\Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{b}} \times \mathrm{W}_{1} \times \mathrm{M}_{2}}$
- **(C)** $\frac{\mathrm{M}_{2} \times \Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{b}} \times \mathrm{W}_{2}}{\mathrm{~W}_{1}}$
- **(D)** $\frac{\Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{b}} \times \mathrm{M}_{2} \times \mathrm{W}_{1}}{\mathrm{~W}_{2}}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $\frac{\Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{b}} \times \mathrm{M}_{2} \times \mathrm{W}_{1}}{\mathrm{~W}_{2}}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{b}}=\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{b}} \mathrm{m}=\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{b}} \times \frac{\mathrm{W}_{2}}{\mathrm{M}_{2} \mathrm{~W}_{1}}$
$\therefore \mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{b}}=\frac{\Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{b}} \times \mathrm{M}_{2} \times \mathrm{W}_{1}}{\mathrm{~W}_{2}}$

</details>

### Q202. MHT CET 2020 (20 Oct Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

What will be the molar mass of solute if vapour pressure of pure benzene is $450 \mathrm{~mm}$ $\mathrm{Hg}$ when $1 \cdot 5 \mathrm{~g}$ of non volatile solute is added to $30 \mathrm{~g}$ of benzene? (Vapour pressure of solution $=400 \mathrm{~mm} \mathrm{Hg}$, atomic mass $\mathrm{C}=12, \mathrm{H}=1$ )

**Options:**

- **(A)** $1.35 \cdot 1 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(B)** $\times 2.26 \cdot 1 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(C)** $\mathrm{X}_{3} .28 \cdot 4 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(D)** $\times$ 4. $30 \cdot 0 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $1.35 \cdot 1 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\mathrm{P}^{0}=450 \mathrm{~mm} \mathrm{~Hg},\quad\mathrm{P}=400 \mathrm{~mm} \mathrm{~Hg}, \quad \mathrm{M}_{1}=78 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$ (molar mass of benzene),
$\mathrm{W}_{1}=30 \mathrm{~g}, \mathrm{~W}_{2}=1.5 \mathrm{~g}, \mathrm{M}_{2}=?$
$\mathrm{M}_{2}=\frac{\mathrm{W}_{2} \mathrm{M}_{1}}{\mathrm{~W}_{1}} \times \frac{\mathrm{P}_{0}}{\left(\mathrm{P}_{0}-\mathrm{P}\right)}$
$\therefore \mathrm{M}_{2}=\frac{1.5 \times 78}{30} \times \frac{450}{(450-400)}=35.1 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$

</details>

### Q203. MHT CET 2020 (20 Oct Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

If van't Hoff factor of monofluoroacetic acid in water is $1 \cdot 076$. What is it's degree of dissociation?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $0.76$
- **(B)** $0.076$
- **(C)** $0.924$
- **(D)** $0.538$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $0.076$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\mathrm{i}=1.076, \quad \alpha=?$
$\mathrm{CH}_{2} \mathrm{FCOOH} \rightleftharpoons \mathrm{CH}_{2} \mathrm{FCOO}^{-}+\mathrm{H}^{+}$
$\therefore \mathrm{n}=2$
$\alpha=\frac{i-1}{n-1}=\frac{1.076-1}{2-1}=0.076$

</details>

### Q204. MHT CET 2020 (19 Oct Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The vapour pressure of solvent decreases by $10 \mathrm{~mm} \mathrm{Hg}$ if mole fraction of non volatile solute is $0.2$ Calculate vapour pressure of solvent.

**Options:**

- **(A)** $50 \mathrm{~mm}$ of $\mathrm{Hg}$
- **(B)** $70 \mathrm{~mm}$ of $\mathrm{Hg}$
- **(C)** $40 \mathrm{~mm}$ of $\mathrm{Hg}$
- **(D)** $60 \mathrm{~mm}$ of $\mathrm{Hg}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $50 \mathrm{~mm}$ of $\mathrm{Hg}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

(A)
$\begin{array}{l}
\mathrm{P}_{0}-\mathrm{P}=10 \mathrm{~mm} \text { of } \mathrm{Hg}, \quad \mathrm{x}_{2}=0.2 \\
\mathrm{P}_{0}=?
\end{array}$
According to Raoult's Law
$\begin{array}{l}
\frac{\mathrm{P}_{0}-\mathrm{P}}{\mathrm{P}_{0}}=\mathrm{x}_{2} \quad \therefore \frac{10 \mathrm{~mm} \text { of } \mathrm{Hg}}{\mathrm{P}_{0}}=0.2 \\
\therefore \mathrm{P}_{0}=50 \mathrm{~mm} \text { of } \mathrm{Hg}
\end{array}$

</details>

### Q206. MHT CET 2020 (19 Oct Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

If boiling point of urea solution is $100 \cdot 18^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$ and $\mathrm{kb}$ for water is $0.512 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$, molality of solution is? (Boiling point of water $=100^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$ )

**Options:**

- **(A)** $0 \cdot 25 \mathrm{~mol} \mathrm{~kg}^{-1}$
- **(B)** $0 \cdot 6 \mathrm{~mol} \mathrm{~kg}^{-1}$
- **(C)** $0 \cdot 45 \mathrm{~mol} \mathrm{~kg}^{-1}$
- **(D)** $0 \cdot 35 \mathrm{~mol} \mathrm{~kg}^{-1}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $0 \cdot 35 \mathrm{~mol} \mathrm{~kg}^{-1}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

(D)
$\Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{b}}=(100.18+273)-(100+273)=0.18 \mathrm{~K}$
$\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{b}}=0.512 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}, \mathrm{~m}=?$
$\Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{b}}=\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{b}} \times \mathrm{m} \quad \therefore \mathrm{m}=\frac{\Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{b}}}{\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{b}}}$
$\therefore \mathrm{m}=\frac{0.18 \mathrm{~K}}{0.512 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}}=0.35 \mathrm{~mol} \mathrm{~kg}^{-1}$

</details>

Relative lowering in vapour pressure of a solution containing non volatile solute is the ratio of

- **(D)** Number of moles of solute to total number of moles of solution.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) Number of moles of solute to total number of moles of solution.**

**Detailed Solution:**

(A)

</details>

### Q208. MHT CET 2020 (16 Oct Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Solutions $A, B, C$ and $D$ are respectively $0 \cdot 2$ M urea, $0 \cdot 10$ M $\mathrm{NaCl}, 0.05 \mathrm{M} \mathrm{BaCl}_{2}$ and $0.05 \mathrm{M} \mathrm{AlCl}_{3}$.
All solutions are isotonic with each other except

**Options:**

- **(A)** B
- **(B)** A
- **(C)** D
- **(D)** C

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) C**

**Detailed Solution:**

(A)
Osmotic pressure, $\pi=$ iMRT
(A) $\pi_{\text {Urea }}=1 \times 0.2 \mathrm{RT}=0.2 \mathrm{RT}$
(B) $\pi_{\mathrm{NaCl}}=2 \times 0.1 \mathrm{RT}=0.2 \mathrm{RT}$
(C) $\pi_{\mathrm{BaCl}_{2}}=3 \times 0.05 \mathrm{RT}=0.15 \mathrm{RT}$
(D) $\pi_{\text {ACC }_{3}}=4 \times 0.05 \mathrm{RT}=0.2 \mathrm{RT}$
$\therefore$ All solutions are isotonic with each other except $\mathrm{C}$.

</details>

- **(D)** 12.3 atm

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) 12.3 atm**

**Detailed Solution:**

(C)
For a semimolar solution, Molarity $(M)=0.5$
$\pi=\mathrm{MRT}=0.5 \mathrm{~mol} \mathrm{~L}^{-} \times 0.082 \mathrm{~L} \mathrm{~atm} \mathrm{~K}^{-1} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1} \times 300 \mathrm{~K}=12.3 \mathrm{~atm}$

</details>

### Q210. MHT CET 2020 (16 Oct Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A solution has an osmotic pressure of 'x' kPa at $300 \mathrm{~K}$ having 1 mole of solute in $10 \cdot 5 \mathrm{~m}^{3}$ of solution. If it's osmotic pressure is reduced to $\left(\frac{1}{10}\right)^{\text {th }}$ of it's initial value, what is the new volume of solution?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $30 \mathrm{~m}^{3}$
- **(B)** $105 \mathrm{~m}^{3}$
- **(C)** $110 \mathrm{~m}^{3}$
- **(D)** $11.0 \mathrm{~m}^{3}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $105 \mathrm{~m}^{3}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

(A)
$\begin{array}{l}
\pi=\mathrm{X} \mathrm{kPa}, \quad \mathrm{V}=10.5 \mathrm{~m}^{3}, \pi^{\prime}=\frac{\mathrm{X}}{10} \mathrm{kPa}, \quad \mathrm{V}^{\prime}=? \\
\pi=\frac{\mathrm{n}}{\mathrm{v}} \mathrm{RT}
\end{array}$
i. $X=\frac{1}{10.5} \times 8.314 \times 300$;
ii. $\frac{X}{10}=\frac{1}{V^{\prime}} \times 8.314 \times 300$
By dividing equation (i) by (ii)
$\begin{aligned}
\frac{X}{X / 10} &=\frac{\frac{1}{10.5} \times 8.314 \times 300}{\frac{1}{1 / V^{\prime} \times 8.314 \times 300}} \\
10 &=\frac{V^{\prime}}{10.5} \quad \therefore V^{\prime}=105 \mathrm{~m}^{3}
\end{aligned}$

</details>

---

### Q211. MHT CET 2020 (16 Oct Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

If a centimolal aqueous solution of $\mathrm{K}_{3}\left[\mathrm{~F}_{\mathrm{e}}(\mathrm{CN})_{6}\right]$ has degree of dissociation $0 \cdot 78$, what is the value of vant Hoff factor?

**Options:**

- **(A)** 3.34
- **(B)** 1.2
- **(C)** 2.5
- **(D)** 4.0

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) 3.34**

**Detailed Solution:**

(A)
$\mathrm{K}_{3}\left[\mathrm{Fe}(\mathrm{CN})_{6}\right] \longrightarrow 3 \mathrm{~K}^{+}+\left[\mathrm{Fe}(\mathrm{CN})_{6}\right]^{-}$
$\therefore \mathrm{n}=4$
van't Hoff factor (i) $=1+\alpha(n-1)=1+0.78(4-1)=3.34$

</details>

### Q212. MHT CET 2020 (15 Oct Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Identify the correct relation between depression in freezing point and freezing point of pure solvent?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\mathrm{T}^{\circ}=\mathrm{T} \times \Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{f}}$
- **(B)** $\mathrm{T}^{\circ}=\Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{f}}-\mathrm{T}$
- **(C)** $\mathrm{T}^{\circ}=\mathrm{T}-\Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{f}}$
- **(D)** $\mathrm{T}^{\circ}=\Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{f}}+\mathrm{T}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $\mathrm{T}^{\circ}=\Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{f}}+\mathrm{T}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\Delta T_{f}=T^{0}-T$
$\therefore T^{0}=\Delta T_{f}+T$
$T^{0}=$ freezing point of pure solvent
$T=$ freezing point of solution
$\Delta T_{f}=$ depression in freezing point

</details>

### Q215. MHT CET 2020 (14 Oct Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

What is the value of $\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{f}}$ if $30 \mathrm{~g}$ urea (molar mass 60 ) dissolved in $0.5 \mathrm{dm}^{3}$ of water decreases freezing point by $0 \cdot 15^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$ ?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $0 \cdot 15 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(B)** $0.030 \mathrm{Kkg} \mathrm{mol}^{-1}$
- **(C)** $0.30 \mathrm{Kkg} \mathrm{mol}^{-1}$
- **(D)** $0.015 \mathrm{Kkg} \mathrm{mol}^{-1}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $0 \cdot 15 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Mass of water $=0.5 \mathrm{dm}^{3}=0.5 \mathrm{~kg}$ Moles of urea $=\frac{30}{60}=0.5$ mole
Molality of urea $=\frac{0.5 \mathrm{~mol}}{0.5 \mathrm{~kg}}=1 \mathrm{~mol} / \mathrm{kg}$
$\Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{f}}=\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{f}} \times \mathrm{m}$
$\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{f}}=\frac{\Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{f}}}{\mathrm{m}}=\frac{0.15 \mathrm{~K}}{1 \mathrm{~mol} / \mathrm{kg}}=0.15 \mathrm{Kmol}^{-1} \mathrm{~kg}$

</details>

---

### Q216. MHT CET 2020 (14 Oct Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

Vapour pressure of solvent 'A' is $0.90 \mathrm{~atm}$, when a non volatile solute is added, vapour pressure drops to $0.60 \mathrm{~atm}$, what is mole fraction of $\mathrm{A}$ in solution?

**Options:**

- **(A)** 0.300
- **(B)** 0.333
- **(C)** 0.500
- **(D)** 0.667

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) 0.667**

**Detailed Solution:**

$$
\begin{array}{l}
\frac{P_{0}-P}{P_{0}}=x_{2} \\
\frac{0.9-0.6}{0.9}=0.333
\end{array}
$$
Now, $x_{1}+x_{2}=1$
$$
\therefore \quad x_{1}=1-x_{2}=1-0.333=0.667
$$

</details>

### Q218. MHT CET 2020 (13 Oct Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

$0.5$ molal aqueous solution of a weak acid $(\mathrm{HX})$ is $20 \%$. ionised. If $\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{f}}$ of water is $1.86 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$, the lowering in freezing point of solution is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $-0 \cdot 56 \mathrm{~K}$
- **(B)** $-1 \cdot 12 \mathrm{~K}$
- **(C)** $1 \cdot 12 \mathrm{~K}$
- **(D)** $0.56 \mathrm{~K}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $1 \cdot 12 \mathrm{~K}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\mathrm{HX} \rightleftharpoons \mathrm{H}^{+}+\mathrm{X}^{-}$
For dissociation of an electrolyte,
$\alpha=\frac{i-1}{n-1} \quad \therefore 0.2=\frac{i-1}{2-1}$
$\therefore \mathrm{i}-1=0.2$
$\therefore \mathrm{i}=0.2+1=1.2$
Now, $\Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{f}}=\mathrm{i} \mathrm{k}_{\mathrm{f}} \mathrm{m}=1.2 \times 1.86 \times 0.5=1.12 \mathrm{~K}$

</details>

### Q222. MHT CET 2019 (02 May Shift 1)

Calculate Vant Hoff factor for <math><mn>0.2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"></mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math> aqueous solution of <math><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi></math> which freezes at <math><mo>-</mo><msup><mrow><mn>0.680</mn></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>.</mo><mi mathvariant="normal"></mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>1.86</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"></mi><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"></mi><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"></mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced></math>

### Q223. MHT CET 2019 (Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Which of the following sets of solutions of urea <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>m</mi><mi>o</mi><mi>l</mi><mo>.</mo><mi>m</mi><mi>a</mi><mi>s</mi><mi>s</mi><mtext> </mtext><mn>60</mn><mtext> </mtext><mi>g</mi><mtext> </mtext><mi>m</mi><mi>o</mi><msup><mrow><mi>l</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced></math> and sucrose <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>m</mi><mi>o</mi><mi>l</mi><mo>.</mo><mi>m</mi><mi>a</mi><mi>s</mi><mi>s</mi><mtext> </mtext><mn>342</mn><mtext> </mtext><mi>g</mi><mtext> </mtext><mi>m</mi><mi>o</mi><msup><mrow><mi>l</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced></math> is isotonic?

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mn>9.1</mn><mtext> </mtext><mi>g</mi><msup><mrow><mi>L</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> urea and <math><mtext> </mtext><mn>6.0</mn><mi>g</mi><msup><mrow><mi>L</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> sucrose
- **(B)** <math><mn>3.0</mn><mtext> </mtext><mi>g</mi><msup><mrow><mi>L</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mtext> </mtext></math> urea and <math><mn>3.0</mn><mi>g</mi><msup><mrow><mi>L</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> sucrose
- **(C)** <math><mn>6.0</mn><mi>g</mi><msup><mrow><mi>L</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mtext>   </mtext></math> urea and <math><mtext> </mtext><mn>9.0</mn><mi>g</mi><msup><mrow><mi>L</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> sucrose
- **(D)** <math><mn>3.0</mn><mi>g</mi><msup><mrow><mi>L</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mtext> </mtext></math> urea and <math><mn>17.1</mn><mi>g</mi><msup><mrow><mi>L</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> sucrose

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math><mn>3.0</mn><mi>g</mi><msup><mrow><mi>L</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mtext> </mtext></math> urea and <math><mn>17.1</mn><mi>g</mi><msup><mrow><mi>L</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> sucrose**

**Detailed Solution:**

<strong>Key Idea</strong> Isotonic solutions are those solutions which have same osmotic pressure at a given temperature. 
Formula for osmotic pressure, <math><mi>π</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>C</mi><mi>R</mi><mi>T</mi></math>  
Considering the set given in option <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>,</mo></math> i.e. <math><mn>3.0</mn><mi>g</mi><msup><mrow><mi>L</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> urea and 17.1 <math><mi>g</mi><msup><mrow><mi>L</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> sucrose. Given, molecular of sucrose 342 <math><mi>g</mi><mi>m</mi><mi>o</mi><msup><mrow><mi>l</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>.</mo></math>  
For urea, 
Conc.C <math><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>60</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>20</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>  
Osmotic pressure <math><msub><mrow><mi>π</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>20</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>×</mo><mi>R</mi><mo>×</mo><mi>T</mi></math>  
For sucrose conc. <math><mi>C</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>17.1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>342</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>20</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>  
<math><mo>∴</mo></math> Osmotic pressure, <math><msub><mrow><mi>π</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>20</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>R</mi><mi>T</mi></math>  
Thus, set of solution of urea and sucrose given in option <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> is isotonic.

</details>

### Q224. MHT CET 2019 (Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Relationship between van’t Hoff’s factor (i) and degree of dissociation <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>α</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mi>i</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>α</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mi>n</mi><mi>'</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math><mi>i</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>α</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>n</mi><mi>'</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math><mi>α</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>i</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>n</mi><mi>'</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** <math><mi>α</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>i</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mi>n</mi><mi>'</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math><mi>α</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>i</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mi>n</mi><mi>'</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Relationship between vant Hoff factor (i) and degree of dissociation <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>α</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> is given by 
<math><mi>α</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>i</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>n</mi><mi>'</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>  
Where, n is the number of ions formed after dissociation. The relationship can be obtained as follows: For the reaction, <math><mi>A</mi><mo>⇌</mo><mi>n</mi><mi>'</mi><mi>B</mi></math>  
Initially 1 mole 0 
After dissociation <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>α</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> mole n’ <math><mi>α</mi></math>  
Total number of moles present in the solution 
<math><mo>=</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>α</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><msup><mrow><mi>n</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>'</mi></mrow></msup><mi>α</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>n</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>'</mi></mrow></msup><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mi>α</mi></math>  
Van’t Hoff factor, <math><mi>i</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>n</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>'</mi></mrow></msup><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mi>α</mi><mo>&gt;</mo></math> 1 if <math><mi>n</mi><mi>'</mi><mo>≥</mo><mn>2</mn></math>  
<math><mo>∴</mo><mtext>                </mtext><mi>α</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>i</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mi>n</mi><mi>'</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>

</details>

---

### Q225. MHT CET 2019 (Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

18 gram glucose (Molar mass = 180) is dissolved in 100 mol of water at 300 K. If R = 3 <math><mn>0.0821</mn><mtext> </mtext><mi>L</mi><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mtext> </mtext><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><msup><mrow><mi>K</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> what is the osmotic pressure of solution?

**Options:**

- **(A)** 2.463 atm
- **(B)** 24.63 atm
- **(C)** 8.21 atm
- **(D)** 0.821 atm

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) 24.63 atm**

**Detailed Solution:**

The various quantities known to us are as follows: 
<math><mi>R</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0.0821</mn><mtext> </mtext><mi>L</mi><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mtext> </mtext><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><msup><mrow><mi>K</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>  
<math><msub><mrow><mi>w</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>18</mn><mtext> </mtext><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>  
Molar mass <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>M</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>180</mn></math>  
<math><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>300</mn><mtext> </mtext><mi>K</mi></math>  
<math><mi>V</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>100</mn><mtext> </mtext><mi>m</mi><mi>L</mi></math>  
To calculate the osmotic pressure of solution, we use the following formula, 
<math><mi>π</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>w</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mi>R</mi><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>M</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mi>V</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>18</mn><mo>×</mo><mn>0.0821</mn><mo>×</mo><mn>300</mn><mo>×</mo><mn>1000</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>180</mn><mo>×</mo><mn>100</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>  
<math><mo>=</mo><mn>24.63</mn><mtext> </mtext><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>

</details>

---

### Q226. MHT CET 2019 (Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The elevation in boiling point of 2.25 mole aqueous solution of a substance is 
<math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>K</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>b</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>0.52</mn><mtext> </mtext><mi>K</mi><mtext> </mtext><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mtext> </mtext><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced></math>

**Options:**

- **(A)** 0.5 K
- **(B)** 0.50 K
- **(C)** 0.13 K
- **(D)** 2.08 K

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) 0.13 K**

**Detailed Solution:**

<strong>Key Idea</strong> Elevation in boiling is directly proportional to molal concentration of solute in a solution <math><mi>Δ</mi><msub><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mo>∝</mo><mi>Δ</mi><msub><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>b</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mrow><mi>K</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>b</mi></mrow></msub><mi>m</mi></math>  
The elevation in boiling point <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>Δ</mi><msub><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>b</mi></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></math> of 0.25 molal aqueous solution of a substance is 
<math><mi>Δ</mi><msub><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>b</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mrow><mi>K</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>b</mi></mrow></msub><mo>×</mo><mi>m</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0.52</mn><mo>×</mo><mn>0.25</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0.13</mn><mtext>  </mtext><mi>K</mi></math>

</details>

---

### Q227. MHT CET 2018

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The correct relation between the elevation in boiling point and the molar mass of a solute is: (<math> <mrow> <msub> <mi>W</mi> <mn>1</mn></msub></mrow></math> and <math> <msub> <mi>W</mi> <mn>2</mn> </msub> </math> are respectively weight of solvent &amp; solute)

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math> <mrow>  <msub>   <mtext>M</mtext>   <mn>2</mn>  </msub>  <mo>=</mo><mfrac>   <mrow>    <msub>     <mtext>K</mtext>     <mtext>b</mtext>    </msub>    <mo>&#x00D7;</mo><msub>     <mtext>W</mtext>     <mn>2</mn>    </msub>    <mo>&#x00D7;</mo><mn>1000</mn></mrow>   <mrow>    <msub>     <mrow>      <mtext>&#x0394;T</mtext></mrow>     <mtext>b</mtext>    </msub>    <mo>&#x00D7;</mo><msub>     <mtext>W</mtext>     <mn>1</mn>    </msub>    </mrow>  </mfrac>  </mrow></math>
- **(B)** <math> <mrow>  <msub>   <mtext>M</mtext>   <mn>2</mn>  </msub>  <mo>=</mo><mfrac>   <mrow>    <msub>     <mtext>K</mtext>     <mtext>b</mtext>    </msub>    <mo>&#x00D7;</mo><msub>     <mtext>W</mtext>     <mn>1</mn>    </msub>    <mo>&#x00D7;</mo><mn>1000</mn></mrow>   <mrow>    <msub>     <mrow>      <mtext>&#x0394;T</mtext></mrow>     <mtext>b</mtext>    </msub>    <mo>&#x00D7;</mo><msub>     <mtext>W</mtext>     <mn>2</mn>    </msub>    </mrow>  </mfrac>  </mrow></math>
- **(C)** <math> <mrow>  <msub>   <mtext>M</mtext>   <mn>2</mn>  </msub>  <mo>=</mo><mfrac>   <mrow>    <msub>     <mrow>      <mtext>&#x0394;T</mtext></mrow>     <mtext>b</mtext>    </msub>    <mo>&#x00D7;</mo><msub>     <mtext>K</mtext>     <mtext>b</mtext>    </msub>    <mo>&#x00D7;</mo><mn>1000</mn></mrow>   <mrow>    <msub>     <mtext>W</mtext>     <mn>1</mn>    </msub>    <mo>&#x00D7;</mo><msub>     <mtext>W</mtext>     <mn>2</mn>    </msub>    </mrow>  </mfrac>  </mrow></math>
- **(D)** <math> <mrow>  <msub>   <mtext>M</mtext>   <mn>2</mn>  </msub>  <mo>=</mo><mfrac>   <mrow>    <msub>     <mrow>      <mtext>&#x0394;T</mtext></mrow>     <mtext>b</mtext>    </msub>    <mo>&#x00D7;</mo><msub>     <mtext>W</mtext>     <mn>1</mn>    </msub>    <mo>&#x00D7;</mo><mn>1000</mn></mrow>   <mrow>    <msub>     <mtext>K</mtext>     <mtext>b</mtext>    </msub>    <mo>&#x00D7;</mo><msub>     <mtext>W</mtext>     <mn>2</mn>    </msub>    </mrow>  </mfrac>  </mrow></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math> <mrow>  <msub>   <mtext>M</mtext>   <mn>2</mn>  </msub>  <mo>=</mo><mfrac>   <mrow>    <msub>     <mtext>K</mtext>     <mtext>b</mtext>    </msub>    <mo>&#x00D7;</mo><msub>     <mtext>W</mtext>     <mn>2</mn>    </msub>    <mo>&#x00D7;</mo><mn>1000</mn></mrow>   <mrow>    <msub>     <mrow>      <mtext>&#x0394;T</mtext></mrow>     <mtext>b</mtext>    </msub>    <mo>&#x00D7;</mo><msub>     <mtext>W</mtext>     <mn>1</mn>    </msub>    </mrow>  </mfrac>  </mrow></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math> <mrow> <mo>Δ</mo> <msub> <mtext>T</mtext> <mtext>b</mtext> </msub> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>K</mtext> <mtext>b</mtext> </msub> <mo>×</mo> <msub> <mtext>W</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msub> <mo>×</mo> <mn>1</mn> <mn>0</mn> <mn>0</mn> <mn>0</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>W</mtext> <mn>1</mn> </msub> <mo>×</mo> <msub> <mtext>M</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msub> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>
<math> <mrow>  <msub>   <mtext>M</mtext>   <mn>2</mn>  </msub>  <mo>=</mo><mfrac>   <mrow>    <msub>     <mtext>K</mtext>     <mtext>b</mtext>    </msub>    <mo>&#x00D7;</mo><msub>     <mtext>W</mtext>     <mn>2</mn>    </msub>    <mo>&#x00D7;</mo><mn>1000</mn></mrow>   <mrow>    <msub>     <mrow>      <mtext>&#x0394;T</mtext></mrow>     <mtext>b</mtext>    </msub>    <mo>&#x00D7;</mo><msub>     <mtext>W</mtext>     <mn>1</mn>    </msub>    </mrow>  </mfrac>  </mrow></math>

</details>

### Q228. MHT CET 2017

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

For which among the following equimolar aqueous solutions Van't Hoff factor has the lowest value?

**Options:**

- **(A)** Aluminium Chloride
- **(B)** Potassium Sulphate
- **(C)** Ammonium Chloride
- **(D)** Urea

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) Urea**

**Detailed Solution:**

Urea is non-electrolyte and it&#39;s Van&#39;t hoff factor <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>)</mo></math> is <math> <mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo> </math> While other are electrolyte, So their <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>)</mo></math> values are
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>AlCl</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>SO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><mi>Cl</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn></math>

</details>

### Q229. MHT CET 2017

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The osmotic pressure of solution containing 34.2 g of cane sugar (molar mass = <math> <mrow> <mn>342</mn><mtext> </mtext><mtext>g</mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext>m</mtext><mtext>o</mtext><msup> <mtext>l</mtext> <mrow> <mo>−</mo><mn>1</mn> </mrow> </msup> </mrow> </math> in 1L of solution at <math><msup><mrow><mn>20</mn></mrow><mrow><mtext>o</mtext></mrow></msup></math> C is <math><mo>(</mo><mtext mathvariant="normal">G</mtext><mtext mathvariant="normal">i</mtext><mtext mathvariant="normal">v</mtext><mtext mathvariant="normal">e</mtext><mtext mathvariant="normal">n</mtext><mo>,</mo><mtext> </mtext><mtext>R</mtext><mo>=</mo><mn>0.082</mn><mtext> </mtext><mtext>L</mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext>a</mtext><mtext>t</mtext><mtext>m</mtext><mtext> </mtext><msup><mrow><mtext>K</mtext></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mtext> </mtext><mtext>m</mtext><mtext>o</mtext><msup><mrow><mtext>l</mtext></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>)</mo></math>

**Options:**

- **(A)** 2.40 atm
- **(B)** 3.6 atm
- **(C)** 24 atm
- **(D)** 0.0024 atm

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Detailed Solution:**

<math><mi>π</mi><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math> 
 <math><mi>π</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>34.2</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>342</mn><mo>×</mo><mn>1</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>×</mo><mn>0.082</mn><mo>×</mo><mn>293</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>2.40</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>

</details>

### Q230. MHT CET 2016

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Identify the compound amongst the following whose 0.1 M aqueous solution has the highest boiling point.

**Options:**

- **(A)** Glucose
- **(B)** Sodium chloride
- **(C)** Calcium chloride
- **(D)** Ferric chloride

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) Ferric chloride**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math> <mrow>  <mi>&#x0394;</mi><msub>   <mi>T</mi>   <mi>b</mi>  </msub>  <mo>=</mo><msub>   <mi>k</mi>   <mi>b</mi>  </msub>  <mo>&#x00D7;</mo><mi>m</mi><mo>&#x00D7;</mo><mi>i</mi></mrow></math>
Since they all have the same concentration, the one that will break into most parts has the highest boiling Points. Ionic compounds will ionize.
<math><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mo>:</mo><mn>1</mn></math> part (covalent, does not ionize)
<math><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mo>:</mo><mn>2</mn></math> parts <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>1</mn><mi> </mi><mi>N</mi><mi>a</mi><mo>,</mo><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mn>1</mn><mi>C</mi><mi>l</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>
<math><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mo>:</mo><mn>3</mn></math> parts <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>1</mn><mi> </mi><mi>C</mi><mi>a</mi><mo>,</mo><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mn>2</mn><mi> </mi><mi>C</mi><mi>l</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>
<math><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mo>:</mo><mn>4</mn></math> parts <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>1</mn><mi> </mi><mi>F</mi><mi>e</mi><mo>,</mo><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mn>3</mn><mi> </mi><mi>C</mi><mi>l</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>
Hence, the correct choice is Ferric chloride.

</details>

### Q231. MHT CET 2016

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The equation that represents the van't Hoff general solution equation is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mfrac><mi>RT</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>nRT</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi></mfrac><mi>RT</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>nVRT</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mfrac><mi>RT</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

The equation that represents the van't Hoff general solution equation is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">π</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mfrac><mi>RT</mi></math>.

</details>

### Q232. MHT CET 2011

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

$34.2 \mathrm{~g}$ of cane sugar is dissolved in $180 \mathrm{~g}$ of water. The relative lowering of vapour pressure will he

**Options:**

- **(A)** $0.0099$
- **(B)** $1.1597$
- **(C)** $0.840$
- **(D)** $0.9901$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $0.0099$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\frac{p^{\circ}-p_{S}}{p^{\circ}}=\frac{\frac{w_{2}}{m_{2}}}{\frac{w_{1}}{m_{1}}+\frac{w_{2}}{M_{2}}}$
$=\frac{\frac{34.2}{342}}{\frac{34.2}{342}+\frac{180}{18}}$
$=\frac{0.1}{10.1}$
$=0.0099$

</details>

### Q233. MHT CET 2010

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Dissolution of $1.5 \mathrm{~g}$ of a non-volatile solute (mol. wt. $=60$ ) in $250 \mathrm{~g}$ of a solvent reduces its freezing point by $0.01{ }^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$. Find the molal depression constant of the solvent.

**Options:**

- **(A)** $0.01$
- **(B)** $0.001$
- **(C)** $0.0001$
- **(D)** $0.1$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $0.1$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Depression in freezing point,
$$
\begin{array}{c}
\Delta T_{f}=k_{f} \times m \\
\text { where, } m=\text { molality }=\frac{\text { wt. of solute } \times 1000}{\text { mol. wt. of solute }}
\end{array}
$$
$\times$ wt. of solvent
$$
\begin{aligned}
&=\frac{1.5 \times 1000}{60 \times 250} \\
&=0.1 \\
\Rightarrow \quad \Delta T_{f} &=k_{f} \times 0.1 \\
0.01 &=k_{f} \times 0.1 \\
\therefore \quad k_{f} &=\frac{0.01}{0.1} \\
&=0.1{ }^{\circ} \mathrm{C} \text { molal }^{-1}
\end{aligned}
$$

</details>

### Q234. MHT CET 2010

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Ratio of loss in solvent to gain in $\mathrm{CaCl}_{2}$ tube is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{p^{\circ}}{p}$
- **(B)** $\frac{p}{p^{\circ}}$
- **(C)** $\frac{p^{\circ}-p}{p^{\circ}}$
- **(D)** $\frac{p-p^{\circ}}{p}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $\frac{p^{\circ}-p}{p^{\circ}}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\because$ Loss in the weight of solvent $=p^{\circ}-p$
$\because$ Gain in the weight of $\mathrm{CaCl}_{2}$ tube $=p^{\circ}$ $\Rightarrow \frac{\text { Loss in the weight of solvent }}{\text { Gain in the weight of } \mathrm{CaCl}_{2} \text { tube }}=\frac{p^{\circ}-p}{p^{\circ}}$

</details>

### Q235. MHT CET 2009

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Which of the following is not a colligative property?

**Options:**

- **(A)** Elevation in boiling point
- **(B)** Lowering of vapour pressure
- **(C)** Osmotic pressure
- **(D)** Freezing point

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) Freezing point**

**Detailed Solution:**

Depression in freezing point is a colligative property but feezing point is not a colligative property.

</details>

### Q236. MHT CET 2009

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Maximum depression in freezing point is caused by

**Options:**

- **(A)** potassium chloride
- **(B)** sodium sulphate
- **(C)** magnesium sulphate
- **(D)** magnesium carbonate

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) sodium sulphate**

**Detailed Solution:**

Depression in freezing point is a colligative property, so depends upon the number of particles. Among, potassium chloride (KCl), sodium $\quad$ sulphate $\quad\left(\mathrm{Na}_{2} \mathrm{SO}_{4}\right), \quad$ magnesium sulphate $\left(\mathrm{MgSO}_{4}\right)$ and magnesium carbonate $\left(\mathrm{MgCO}_{3}\right), \mathrm{Na}_{2} \mathrm{SO}_{4}$ produces maximum number
of particles.
$\mathrm{Na}_{2} \mathrm{SO}_{4} \rightleftharpoons \underbrace{2 \mathrm{Na}^{+}+\mathrm{SO}_{4}^{2-}}_{3 \text { particles }}$
Thus, it causes maximum depression in freezing point.

</details>

### Q237. MHT CET 2008

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Which of the following solutions will have highest boiling point?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $0.1 \mathrm{M} \mathrm{FeCl}_{3}$
- **(B)** $0.1 \mathrm{M} \mathrm{BaCl}_{2}$
- **(C)** $0.1 \mathrm{M} \mathrm{NaCl}$
- **(D)** $0.1 \mathrm{M}$ urea $\left(\mathrm{NH}_{2} \mathrm{CONH}_{2}\right)$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $0.1 \mathrm{M} \mathrm{FeCl}_{3}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Elevation in boiling point is a colligative property, ie, depends only on number of particles or ions. $0.1 \mathrm{M} \mathrm{FeCl}_{3}$ gives maximum number of ions, thus has highest boiling point.

</details>
